āṟāvatu -  viṉaiyiyaṉ muṟṟum.


iṭaiyiyal
iṭaiccoṟkaḷiṉ ilakkaṇamuṇarttiṉamaiyāl iṭaiyiyaleṉṉum peyarttāyiṟṟu. peyaraiyum viṉaiyaiyum cārntu tōṟṟutaliṉ avaṟṟiṉpiṉ kūṟappaṭṭatu moḻikku muṉṉum piṉṉum varumāyiṉum perumpāṉmaiyum iṭaivarutaliṉ iṭaiccollāyiṟṟu eṉpar cēṉāvaraiyar. iṭaiccollāvatu peyarum viṉaiyumpōlat taṉittaṉiyē poruḷuṇara uccarikkappaṭātu peyar viṉaikaḷaic cārntu pulappaṭumeṉṟum peyarum viṉaiyum iṭamākaniṉṟu poruḷuṇarttaliṉ iṭaiccollāyiṟṟeṉṟum kūṟuvar teyvaccilaiyār. poruḷaiyum poruḷatu puṭai peyarcciyaiyun tammālaṉṟit tattaṅkuṟippāluṇarttuñ coṟkaḷ peyarccol viṉaiccoṟkaḷumākātu avaṟṟiṉ vēṟumākātu iṭainikaraṉavāy niṟṟaliṉ iṭaiccolleṉap paṭṭaṉa eṉpar civañāṉa muṉivar. ivviyaliluḷḷa cūttiraṅkaḷ 48. ivaṟṟai 47-ākak koḷvar teyvaccilaiyār.
iṭaiccolleṉṟu collappaṭuvaṉa peyarum viṉaiyum uṇarttum poruḷaic cārntuniṉṟu avaṟṟaiyē veḷippaṭuttu niṟṟalallatu tamakkeṉa vēṟu poruḷillātaṉa eṉpar āciriyar. eṉavē avai poruḷuṇarttumvaḻip peyarp poruṇmai yuṇarttiyum viṉaipporuṇmai yuṇarttiyum varuvaṉa eṉpatu peṟappaṭum.
oru collōṭu oru col puṇarntiyalum vaḻi apporuḷ nilaikku utaviyāki varuvaṉavum, viṉaic coṟkaḷai muṭikkumiṭattu accollakattuk kālaṅkāṭṭum uṟuppu mutaliyaṉavāy niṟpaṉavum, vēṟṟumaiyurupukaḷāy varuvaṉavum, tamakkeṉap poruḷiṉṟic cārttic collappaṭum acainilaikaḷāy niṟpaṉavum, icai niṟaikka varuvaṉavum, tattaṅkuṟippāṟ poruḷuṇarttuvaṉavum. oppumaiyuṇarttum uvamavurupukaḷāy varuvaṉavum eṉa iṭaiccoṟkaḷ eḻuvakaippaṭumeṉpar tolkāppiyar. avaṟṟuḷ ‘puṇariyal nilaiyiṭaip poruḷ nilaikkutavuna’ eṉṟatu alvaḻip poruḷukku uriyaṉa ivai vēṟṟumaip poruḷukku uriyaṉa ivaiyeṉa eḷitil uṇarntu koḷḷutaṟku aṟikuṟiyākiya iṉ, vaṟṟu, mutaliya cāriyaikaḷai ivai eḻuttatikārattiṟ collappaṭṭaṉa.
‘viṉai ceyal maruṅkiṟ kālamoṭu varuna’ eṉṟatu viṉaiccol muṭivupeṟumiṭattuk kālaṅkāṭṭiyum pālkāṭṭiyum viṉaiccollakattu uṟuppāy niṟpaṉavaṟṟai. ivai viṉaiyiyaluṭ kūṟappaṭṭaṉa vēṟṟumaip poruḷiṭattu urupāy varum ai, oṭu, ku, iṉ, atu, kaṇ eṉavarum vēṟṟumaiyurupukaḷ vēṟṟumaiyiyaliṟ kūṟappaṭṭaṉa. acainilaiyum icainiṟaiyum tattaṅ kuṟippiṟ poruḷ ceyvaṉavum ākiya mūvakaiyiṭaic coṟkaḷum ivviyaliṉ kaṇṇē uṇarttappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. oppumaiyuṇarttum iṭaic coṟkaḷākiya aṉṉa, āṅku mutaliya uvama urupukaḷ poruḷatikārattu  uvama iyalil viritturaikkappaṭum. ivviṭaiccoṟkaḷ tammāṟ cārappaṭum coṟku muṉṉum piṉṉum varutalum tammīṟutiritalum piṟitōriṭaic col tammuṉvantu cārappeṟutalum ākiya iyalpiṉavām.
ivviyaliṉkaṇ uṇarttappaṭum acainilai, icainiṟai tattaṅkuṟippiṟ poruḷceyvaṉa eṉṉum mūvakaiyiṭaic coṟkaḷuḷ poruḷpuṇar iṭaiccollākiya tattaṅkuṟippiṟ poruḷ ceyvaṉavaṟṟai mutaṟkaṇṇum, poruḷpuṇarā iṭaiccoṟkaḷākiya acainilai icainiṟaikaḷai ataṉ piṉṉarum uṇarttukiṉṟār. tattaṅkuṟippiṟ poruḷceyyum iṭaic coṟkaḷuḷ palaporuḷ kuṟitta iṭaiccoṟkaḷai 4-mutal 12-varaiyuḷḷa cūttiraṅkaḷilum, oruporuḷ kuṟitta iṭaiccoṟkaḷai 13-mutal 21-varaiyuḷḷa cūttiraṅkaḷilum āciriyar eṭuttōtukiṉṟār. avar eṭuttōtiya iṭaiccoṟkaḷuḷ el eṉṉum col ilaṅkutal eṉṉum oru poruḷ kuṟitta iṭaiccollākum. “elleṉpatu uriccol nīrmaittāyiṉum āciriyar iṭaiccollāka otiṉamaiyāṉ iṭaiccol leṉṟu kōṭum” eṉṟār cēṉāvaraiyar. ‘uriccol kuṟaic collāki niṟkum, itu kuṟaiyiṉṟi niṟṟaliṉ iṭaic collāyiṟṟu’ eṉṟār teyvaccilaiyār. acainilaiyākavum icainiṟaiyākavum varum iṭaiccoṟkaḷai 22-mutal 32-varaiyuḷḷa cūttiraṅkaḷāl eṭutturaittār. uyireḻuttukkaḷuḷ oḷakāramallāta neṭilkaḷ āṟum iraṭṭittum aḷapeṭuttum taṉittum iṭaiccoṟkaḷāy niṉṟu ōcaiyālum kuṟippālum poruḷuṇarttum muṟaiyiṉai 38-ām cūttirattilum, naṉṟē, aṉṟē, antō, aṉṉō eṉpavaṟṟiṉ iṟuti niṉṟa ēkāramum ōkāramum kuṟippāṟ poruḷuṇarttu muṟaiyiṉai 34-ām cūttirattilum, mēṟkūṟiya iṭaic colliṉkaṇ ilakkaṇa vēṟupāṭukaḷaiyellām 35-mutal 46-varaiyuḷḷa cūttiraṅ kaḷilum āciriyar virittuk kūṟiyuḷḷār.
mēṟkūṟappaṭṭa iṭaiccoṟkaḷil iccollukku itu poruḷ eṉa nilaipeṟac collappaṭṭaṉavāyiṉum accoṟkaḷiṉ muṉṉum piṉṉum niṉṟa viṉaiyōṭum peyarōṭum iyaittu nōkka accoṟkaḷ muṟkūṟiyavāṟaṉṟi vēṟu poruḷavāyum acainilaiyāyum tirintu vēṟupaṭiṉum avaṟṟiṉ poruḷ nilaiyai ārāyntuṇartal vēṇṭu meṉavum, iṅkeṭutturaitta iṭaiccoṟkaḷēyaṉṟi ivaipōlvaṉa piṟa variṉum avaṟṟaiyum iṅkuc colliya vaṟṟiṉ ilakkaṇattāl uṇarntu vakaippaṭuttik koḷḷutal vēṇṭumeṉavum ivviyaliṟutiyiluḷḷa puṟaṉaṭaic cūttiraṅkaḷāl āciriyar aṟivuṟuttukiṉṟār. itaṉāl āciriyar kālattu vaḻaṅkiya tamiḻccoṟkaḷiṉ parappum iruvakai vaḻakkiṉum coṟkaḷ poruḷuṇarttum neṟiyiṉ virivum iṉitu pulaṉātal kāṇalām.
- ka. veḷḷaivāraṇaṉār 
nūlvaricai 10, pak. 214-216


1ēḻāvatu
iṭaiccelliyal
245.	iṭai eṉappaṭupa peyaroṭum viṉaiyoṭum
	naṭaipeṟ ṟiyalun tamakkiyal pilavē.
eṉpatu eṉ nutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, 2ivvōttu eṉṉa peyarttō eṉiṉ:- iṭaiccolliṉ ilakkaṇam uṇarttutalāṉ iṭaiccollōttu eṉṉum peyarttu. iṭaiccol eṉṉum 3poruṇmai eṉṉai eṉiṉ, peyar viṉaikaḷ uṇarttum poruṭkut 4tāṉ iṭai niṟṟalāṉ iṭaic col āyiṟṟu. iccūttiram eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, iṭaiccoṟku ellām potuvāyatōr ilakkaṇam uṇarttutal nutaliṟṟu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- iṭaiccol eṉṟu collappaṭuvaṉa peyarccollōṭum viṉaic collōṭum vaḻakkup peṟṟu naṭakkum. avvāṟu avaṟṟōṭu naṭattal allatu tāmāka naṭakkum 5iyalpila eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
atu maṉ, varukatillamma eṉavarum.
tamakkiyalpila eṉṟataṉāṉ peyaroṭum viṉai yoṭum aviṭaiccoṟkaḷ varuvaḻic 6coṟpuṟattuvaḻi varutalum, accolluḷ vaḻivarutalum eṉa iruvakaittu eṉpatu peṟappaṭṭatu.
(u-m.) 
varukatil, uṇṭāṉ eṉavarum: ivai viṉai. 
atu maṉ 7maṟṟaiyatu ivai peyar.
maṟṟu, ic cūttirattiṉ poruṇmaiyum ilēciṉ poruṇmaiyum iṭaiccol kiḷaviyum uriccol kiḷaviyum eṉṟa cūttirattuḷḷum ataṉ ilēciṉuḷḷum aṭaṅkumāl eṉiṉ, atu niraṉiṟai vāypāṭṭatākalāṉ 8peyarōṭu iṭaivarum eṉṟum, viṉaiyōṭu urivarum eṉṟum koḷḷak kiṭantamaiyiṉ iṭaiyum iraṇṭōṭu varum 9eṉṟaṟkuk kūṟiṉāṉ eṉpatu. iṉi avilēcu niraṉiṟaic cūttirattatākalāṟ puṟattu vaḻi varutal iṭaiccoṟkākavum, uḷvaḻi varutal uriccoṟkākavum koḷḷak kiṭakkum eṉpatu karuti 10īṇṭum iruvakaiyāṉum iṭaivarum eṉpataṟku 11ilēcu kūṟiṉāṉ pōlum.	(1)
aṭikkuṟippukaḷ
245-1	ei “iṭaiccol”
2 	e “itu ōttu eṉṉa peyarttō”
3 	i “poruṇ yeṉṉai”
4 	i-ii-ei-eii “tāṉ niṟṟalāṉ”
	ei “tāṉ iṭamāka niṟṟalāṉ”
5 	e “iyalpila u-m”
6 	ei “coṟpuṟattavaḻi varutalum colluḷ vaḻi varutalum”
245-7 	ei “maṟṟaiyatu eṉavarum ivai viṉai”
8 	i-ii-ie-ei-eii “peyar eṉappaṭṭu varum eṉṟum”
9	e “eṉpataṟku”
10 	e “iraṇṭum iruvakaiyāṉum”
11 	i “ilaicu kūṟiṉār” (inta iyal muḻuvatum ippiratiyil ilēcu eṉpatu “ilaicu” eṉṟē varukiṟatu. ātaliṉ piṉ ippirati pētam kāṭṭappeṟa villai.)
246.	avai tām,
	puṇariya ṉilaiyiṭaip poruḷnilaik kuta1navum
	viṉaiceyaṉ maruṅkiṉ kālamoṭu varunavum
	vēṟṟumaip poruḷvayiṉ urupā kunavum
	acainilaik kiḷavi āki varunavum
	icainiṟaik kiḷavi āki varunavum
	tattam kuṟippiṉ poruḷcey kunavum
	oppil vaḻiyāṉ poruḷcey kunavumeṉ
	ṟappaṇ piṉavē nuvaluṅ kālai.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, av iṭaic coṟkaḷiṉ pākupāṭu āmāṟu uṇarttutal nutaliṟṟu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- mēl iṭaiccol eṉṟu 2collap paṭṭavaitām, irumoḻi tammiṟ puṇartal iyaṉṟa nilaimaik kaṇ avaṟṟiṉ poruḷnilaimaikku utaviceytu varuvaṉavum, viṉaiccoṟkaḷai muṭikkumiṭattuk kālam kāṭṭum iṭaic coṟkaḷōṭu kūṭit 3tāmpālkāṭṭum coṟkaḷāy varuvaṉavum, vēṟupāṭu ceyta poruṇmaiyiṭattu vēṟṟumai yurupāy varuvaṉavum, vaḻakkiṉkaṇ tamakku ōr poruḷaṉṟit tām aṭainta peyarviṉaikaḷai 4aṭaiyappaṇṇi niṟkum 5nilaimaiyavāy 6varuvaṉavum, ceyyuḷiṭattup poruḷiṉṟi ōrōr 7ōcaiyai niṟaittaṟ poruṇmaiyavāy varuvaṉavum, peyarviṉaikaḷpōla viḷaṅkap poruḷuṇarttāta 8coṟkaḷ tattaṅ kuṟippāṉē oru poruḷai uṇartti varuvaṉavum 9ottal eṉṉum vāypāṭu 10taṉkaṇṇillāk kūṟṟāṉē 11niṉṟa oppumaip poruṇmaiyai uṇarttivaruvaṉavum eṉṟu collappaṭṭa avvilakkaṇaṅkaḷai uṭaiyaṉavām; avaṟṟaic collumkālattu eṉṟavāṟu.
12(u-m.) 
puṇarccikkaṇ varuvaṉa eḻuttōttiṉuḷ 13iṉṉē vaṟṟē eṉṉum cūttirattāṉ ōtappaṭṭaṉa.
iṉit taṉṉiṉamuṭittal eṉpataṉāl kāram, karam, kāṉ eṉṉum eḻuttuc cāriyaiyum koḷḷappaṭum. 
viṉaic 14coṟkaḷ pālkāṭṭi varuvaṉa, viṉaiyiyaluḷ, 15am ām mutalāka  īṟupaṟṟi 16ōtiṉa ellām eṉak koḷka.
iṉi, av viṉaic coṟkaḷ kālamoṭu varuvaṉavum eṉṟataṉāṟ kālamkāṭṭi varuvaṉa, uṇṭāṉ eṉpuḻi iṭaikkaṭ ṭakaramum, uṇṇā niṉṟāṉ eṉpuḻi 17niṉṟum, uṇpāṉ eṉpuḻip pakaramum, attaṉmaiya piṟavum eṉak koḷka. 
kālam uṇartti varutalāṉ avai kālam eṉappaṭṭaṉa.
uṇṭāṉ eṉpuḻik kālaṅkāṭṭum ṭakarattuṭaṉē pāl kāṭṭum 18āṉ kūṭa vantavāṟu aṟika.
ivvāṟu pālum kālamum kāṭṭuvaṉa viṉaikku eṉṟu  19otiṉaṉēlum, ataṉaip perumpāṉmaiyākki uṇṭavaṉ eṉṟāṟpōlap peyarkkum koḷka.
iṉi, oṉṟeṉa muṭittal eṉpataṉāl 20viṉaikkaṭ pāl uṇarttuvaṉavēyaṉṟip peyarkkēuriya vāypāṭṭāl uṇarttuvaṉavum koḷḷappaṭum.
avai nampi, naṅkai eṉṉum ikara aikāra 21īṟṟuppōlvaṉa 22eṉak koḷka. vēṟṟumaiyurupāy varuvaṉa vēṟṟumaiyōttiṉuḷ avai tām 23peyar ai eṉṉum cūttirattuḷ 24peyarai oḻinta ēḻum eṉak koḷka. 
ivaṟṟai 25ōttaṭaivu paṟṟi muṉ kūṟātatu, viṉaikkaṇ varuvaṉa perumpāṉmaiyākal paṟṟip pōlum.
maṟṟai, acainilaiyum, icainiṟaiyum, kuṟippiṟ poruḷ 26ceykunavum ivvōttiṉuḷ tattam ciṟappuc cūttirattuḷḷē utāraṇam kāṭṭappaṭum.
oppil vaḻiyāṟ poruḷ 27ceykunavaṟṟiṟku utāraṇam itaṉ 28piṉṉatikārattuḷ uvamaiyiyaluḷ kāṇappaṭum.
iccūttirattu muṉṉaiya mūṉṟum muṉṉar ōtip pōntaṉa ākalāṉ muṉ vaikkappaṭṭaṉa. piṉṉaiyatu piṉṉati kārattuk kūṟalāl piṉ vaikkappaṭṭatu. iṭaiyaṉa ivvōt tiṉuḷ kūṟappaṭutalāṉ iṭainiṉṟaṉa. āyiṉ iṭaikkaṇ niṉṟa mūṉṟaṉuḷḷum poruḷ ceyvataṉai muṉ 29collātu, acainilai icainiṟaikaḷai muṉ colliyatu eṉṉai eṉiṉ, ivai iraṇṭum oḻiya īṟṟiṉ muṉṉum piṉṉum niṉṟavai ellām 30perumpāṉmaiyiṉavaiyāy niṟṟalāṉum, ivaitām 31avaiyāy niṟṟalāṉum, ivai tām 32avaiyāy niṟṟal ciṟupāṉmai yākalāṉum eṉpatu. 	(2)
aṭikkuṟippukaḷ

247.	avaitām,
	muṉṉum piṉṉum moḻiaṭuttu varutalum
	tammīṟu tiritalum piṟitava ṇilaiyalum
	aṉṉavai ellām uriya eṉpa.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, iṉṉum avaṟṟiṟku āvatōr viti uṇarttutal nutaliṟṟu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- mēl 1vakukkappaṭṭavai tām, muṉṉiṭattum piṉṉiṭattum peyarviṉaiyākiya moḻikaḷai aṭaintu varutalum, accoṟkaḷ tamīṟu oruvaḻi eḻuttu vēṟupaṭṭu varutalum, maṟṟōr iṭaiccol tāṉ niṟkumiṭattē niṟṟalum ākiya attaṉmaiyaiyuṭaiya ilakkaṇam 2ellāvaṟṟiṟkum ilakkaṇamātaṟku uriya eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
muṉṉaṭuttatu: atumaṉ, 3kēṇmiyā eṉpaṉa. 
piṉṉaṭuttatu: koṉṉūr, 4ō otantār eṉpaṉa.
īṟu tirintatu: maṉṉaik kāñci, iḵtottaṉa5eṉpaṉa. 
maṉṉaic col, tillaiccol eṉpaṉavō eṉiṉ, avai poruḷ uṇarttātu coltammai uṇara niṉṟa; āka lāṉ īṇṭaikku ākā eṉpatu. maṟṟu 6eṉṉai tiripu peṟumāṟu eṉiṉ, uṭampoṭu puṇarttal eṉpataṉāṉ avaṟṟai ivvāṟu ōtiya cūttiraṅkaḷāl peṟutum7 eṉpatu.
piṟitavaṇilaiyal: makaviṉai, maṭavai maṉṟamma eṉpatu.		(3)

248.	kaḻivē ākkam oḻiyicaik kiḷavieṉ
	ṟammūṉ ṟeṉpa maṉṉaic collē.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, itu tattam kuṟippiṟ poruḷ ceykuvaṉavaṟṟuḷ 1oṉṟaṉ poruṭ pākupāṭu uṇarttutal nutaliṟṟu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- 2kaḻivup poruṇmaikkaṇ varum maṉṉum, ākkap poruṇmaikkaṇ varum maṉṉum, oḻiyicaip poruṇmaik kaṇ varum maṉṉum eṉa mūṉṟu kūṟṟām eṉpa, maṉ eṉṉum collatu 3poruḷ pāṭṭu vēṟṟumai4 eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
ciṟiya kaṭpeṟiṉē yemak kīyumaṉṉē; itu kaḻivu.
paṇṭukāṭumaṉ: iḵtu ākkam. paṇṭu kāṭu eṉpataṉṟō iṉṟu nāṭu eṉṟu ākkam uṇarttukiṉṟatu eṉiṉ, ataṉ poruḷai ituvuṅ kūṭiniṉṟu uṇarttiṟṟu eṉa uṇarka.
ivvāṟu poruḷ uṇarttalum av iṭaic 5coṟku āvatōr ilakkaṇam 6eṉpatu avvutāraṇam uraiyiṟ koḷḷappaṭum.
paṇṭu 7kūriyatōr vāṇmaṉ: iḵtu oḻiyicai. iṉṟu ōr kuṟaipāṭuṭaittāyiṟṟu eṉṉum col oḻintamai tōṟṟuvittu 8niṉṟamai kāṇka.	(4)

249.	viḻaivē kālam oḻiyicaik kiḷavieṉ
	ṟammūṉ ṟeṉpa tillaic collē.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, ituvum atu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- viḻaiviṉ kaṇṇatum, kālattiṉ kaṇṇatum;  oḻiyicaik kaṇṇatum eṉa mūṉṟu kūṟṟatām 1eṉpa; til eṉṉum iṭaiccol eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
2ciṉmoḻi yarivaiyaip peṟukatil lamma yāṉē; itu viḻaivu.
3peṟṟāṅ kaṟikatil eṉpatu kālam. 
varukatillamma 4em cēri cēra 5eṉpatu oḻiyicai.	(5)

250.	accam payamili kālam perumaieṉ
	ṟappāl nāṉkē koṉṉaic collē.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, ituvum atu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- accattiṉ kaṇṇum, 1payaṉiṉmaik kaṇṇum, kālattiṉ kaṇṇum, perumaik 2kaṇṇum eṉa akkūṟu nāṉkām koṉṉaic col eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
koṉmuṉai yiravūr eṉpatu accam. koṉṉē vantāṉ eṉpatu payaṉiṉmai. 3koṉvaral vāṭai niṉateṉak koṇṭēṉō itu kālam. koṉṉūr tuñciṉum eṉpatu perumai.	(6)

251.	eccam ciṟappē aiyam etirmaṟai
	muṟṟē eṇṇē terinilai ākkameṉ
	ṟappāl eṭṭē ummaic collē.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, ituvum atu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- eccattiṉ kaṇṇatum, ciṟappiṉ kaṇṇatum, aiyattiṉ kaṇṇatum, 1etirmaṟaik kaṇṇatum, muṟṟiṉ 2kaṇṇatum, eṇṇiṉ kaṇṇatum, terinilaik kaṇṇatum, ākkattiṉ kaṇṇatum eṉa 3ikkūṟu eṭṭām um eṉṉum col eṉṟavāṟu.
eccam iṟantatu4 taḻīiyatum, etiratu taḻīiyatum eṉa 5iru vakaittu. 
muṉ cāttaṉ vantāṉ; piṉ koṟṟaṉum vantāṉ eṉpatu iṟantatu taḻīiyiṟṟu.
vēṟu oruvaṉum varum eṉpatu paṭa niṉṟu cāttaṉum varum eṉpatu etiratu taḻīiyiṟṟu.
iṉṉum ivveccam 6muḻuvatum orupuṭai taḻuvuvatum eṉa iruvakaittu.
avaiyāvaṉa:- yāṉ karuvūrkkuc celvēṉ eṉṟāṟku yāṉum avvūrkkup pōtuval eṉpatum, avvāṟu kūṟiṉārkku yāṉum uṟaiyūrkkup 7pōtuvaṉ eṉpatūum eṉa ivai.
iṉic ciṟappu - uyarvu ciṟappum, iḻivu ciṟappum eṉa iru vakaittu.
uyarvu - 8tēvarvēṇṭiṉum vēmpukaikkum eṉpatu.
 ūrkku maṇittē poykai eṉpatum atu.
iḻipu - avvūrp pūcaiyum pulāṟṟiṉṉātu eṉa varum.
aiyam - pattum eṭṭum uḷa eṉpatu.
9ceytatu maṉṟu eṉpatum atu.
etirmaṟai - koṟṟaṉ varutaṟkum uriyaṉ eṉpatu. ivvetir maṟai aḵṟiṇai viravup peyariyalpu māṟuḷavē 10 eṉṟāṟpōlap paṇpu paṟṟiyum varum.
eccattōṭu itaṉiṭai vēṟṟumai eṉṉai eṉiṉ, atu piṟitōr poruḷiṉait taḻuvum, iḵtu apporuṭ ṭāṉum oru kūṟṟait taḻuvum eṉpatu.
iṉi muṟṟu:- tamiḻnāṭṭu mūvēntarum vantār eṉpatu. yātum ūrē 11nāḷumaṉṉāṉ pukaḻumaṉṉai eṉpaṉavum avai.
iṉi eṇ - nilaṉum nīrum eṉpatu. 
iṉit terinilai - 12naṉṟu tītum aṉṟu eṉpatu 13iṭai nikarttatu eṉṟu ōr poruṇmaiyait terivittu 14niṟṟalāṉ terinilai eṉṟu āyiṟṟu.
iṉi ākkam:- neṭiyaṉum valiyaṉum āyiṉāṉ eṉpatu. iḵtu eṇṇaṉṟō eṉiṉ, oruporuḷ taṉṉaiyē 15collu taliṉ aṉṟu āyiṟṟup pōlum.
maṟṟum itaṉ akattu vikaṟpamellām aṟintukoḷka.		(7)

252.	pirinilai viṉāvē etirmaṟai oḻiyicai
	terinilaikkiḷavi ciṟappoṭu tokaii
	irumūṉ ṟeṉpa ōkā rammē.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, ituvum 1atām.
itaṉ poruḷ:- pirinilaip poruṇmai, viṉāp poruṇmai, etirmaṟaip poruṇmai, oḻiyicaip poruṇmai, terinilaip 2poruṇmai ivaṟṟaic ciṟappup poruṇmaiyō3ṭē tokuttu āṟu eṉṟu collupa āciriyar; ōkārattup poruṇmai eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
pirinilai - avaṉō koṇṭāṉ eṉpatu. iḵtu ippōtu viṉāvāy naṭakkiṉṟatu eṉa uṇarka.
iṉi viṉā - avaṉō allaṉō eṉpatu.
iṉi etirmaṟai - yāṉōkoḷvēṉ eṉpatu.
iṉi oḻiyicai - koḷalō koṇṭāṉ eṉpatu.
iṉit terinilai - naṉṟō 4aṉṟu tītō aṉṟu eṉpatu.
iṉic ciṟappu - 5ō o peritu 6eṉpatu.	(8)

253.	1tēṟṟam viṉāvē pirinilai eṇṇē
	īṟṟacai ivvain tēkā rammē.

tēṟṟam :- avaṉē koṇṭāṉ.
5iṉi viṉā :- nīyē koṇṭāy6

254.	viṉaiyē kuṟippē icaiyē paṇpē

	kaṇṇiya nilaittē eṉaeṉ kiḷavi.

The recommendations of the Expert Committee which met at Madras and Thanjavur, examined and selected the manuscripts for publication, were readily accepted by the Government who decided to call these publications “The Madras Government Oriental Series” and appointed the Curator as the General Editor of thereof. Since then, a number of valuable works in various languages and dealing with various subjects has been edited by eminent scholars rioted for their expertness and erudition in the respective spheres of knowledge to which the works belong.

Coming to the present publication Kallatar’s Commentary on Tolkappiyam Collatikaram under the Madras Government Oriental Series, selected by the Expert Committee and sanctioned by the Government on G.O. (Ms.) No. 1947 – Education dated 26th November 1957, its edition was entrusted in the same year to Prof. T.P.Meenakshisundaranar, M.A., M.O.L., B.L., who was then the Chief Professor of Tamil, Presidency College, Madras. Due to a variety of causes which need not to be detailed here, the work could not be brought out earlier than now. That it has taken such a long time to see the light of the day, does, in no way, detract from its merit as an important contribution to the Science of Tamil Grammar.
Of the various languages, the two most ancient and developed ones are Sanskrit and Tamil and the glory of the latter has not a little been enhanced by the monumental work ‘Tolkappiam’ which is more than the earliest extent work on Tamil Grammar, nay, in Tamil itself, since it portrays the colorful and purposeful social life of the Tamils, some hundreds of years ago. Whether the work has been named after the author or vice versa is an interesting point for researcher for going into. Some scholars are of the opinion that Tolkappia might have been so called because hestudied all ancient works (tolkappiankal) before he wrote his magnificent work on Grammar. Some others believe that he belonged to an ancient Brahmin-family called ‘Kappiyakkuti’ in Tamil and since he was a distinguished scion of that family, he came to be respectfully called ‘Tolkappiyar’. That he was the first of the twelve illustrious disciples of Akattiyamuni, styled as the Father of Tamil and his ‘Tolkappiyam’ was but based on his preceptor’s famous work of Grammar, called ‘Perakattiyam’ which has now been unfortunately lost to us, are also points that have not gained the acceptance of a majority of scholars. There is, however, no doubt about the fact that Tolkappiyar must have studied Sanskrit very well and been conversant with the Vedas, the Pratisakhyas, Nirukts etc.,
Researchers on Tolkappiyar’s date have not also had the last word said on it. Peraciriyar says that he was the first student of Akattiyar and this does not help us in any way because the age of that sage has not been finally arrived at. Naccinarkkiniyar, the greatest of commentators, avers that Tolkappiyar wrote his magnum opus towards the end of the first deluge which has not also been determined finally. Nakkirar, the Cankam-celebrity of undaunted courage and conviction, remarks thus “ïÅ  ïil¢r§f¤â‰F« eil¢ r§f¤â‰F«”. The late Maraimalai Atikal has placed him before Panini, the greatest of grammatical geniuses ever known to any language. Internal evidences cannot also be relied upon completely because this work, as the learned Editor is inclined to believe, has not come down to us in its original form. The Professor has quite pertinently aid in his enlightened Introduction thus: “The question may also be raised whether ‘Tolkappiyam’ is the work of one author or is one like ‘Manusmriti’ etc., the work of a family or school of authors.” He also observes that it can be assumed on the basis of the major portions of the work that the Satras of Tolkappiyam belong to the pre-Cankam period. The consensus of scholarly opinion is that Tolkappiyar should be assigned to the 4th Century B.C. it is needless to say that the date of the author is yet another subject where research-scholars have a vast scope for bringing their critical acumen into play and arriving at the truth, according to their lights.
‘Tolkappiyam’ which is said to have had its imprimatur in the learned assembly of Pandian Makirti, under the distinguished presidentship of Atankottacan, and which runs to about 1,600 Sutras, are divided into three Atikaram’s or Sections viz., Eluttatikaram, Collatikaram ad Porulatikaram. While the first section deals with the Phonology of Tamil and the second section reats of Syntax and Morpholgy, the third one comprises the two divisions known as Akam and Puram, which together constitute all the things of the world. The science of Poetics may also be said to be the theme of this section.
While Paripatal, Akananuru, Kalittokai etc., cannot be understood without ‘Tolkappiyam’, the latter, it is no exaggeration to say, cannot be understood without the excellent expositions of versatile commentators like Ilampuranar. The illustrious line of commentators on Tolkappiyam is certainly impressive. It is headed by Ilampuranar, also called ‘Uraiyaciriyar’ by Cenavaraiyar, with the great respect, has alone covered all the three Sections of Tolkappiyam. He must have studied Tamil in the traditional way, sitting at the feet of great masters of Tamil learning and must have had many a commentary before him which has been irretrievably lost to us. The learned Editor rightly observes that this commentator is mainly responsible for the preservation of Tolakappiyam. Cenavaraiyar who is next in the tradition has written an excellent commentary on Collatikaram. He seems to have been very well acquainted with Sanskrit Sciences. Peraciriyar, coming next to him, has commented only upon portions of Porulatikaram, Naccinarkkiniyar, the profile commentator of admirable erudition, seems to have written a commentary on all the three Sections, though that on the last three chapters of Porulatikaram has not yet come to light. The commentary of Teyvaccilaiyar who is chronologically later than Cenavaraiyar and Naccinarkkiniyar on Collatikaram is as lucid as it is instructive.
Then comes Kallatar whose commentary constitutes the present edition. His name and date have been very ably discussed by the Professor who has afforded much food for thought unto research – scholars. He is of the opinion that the present commentary may not represent the work of one particular individual known as Kallatar but may be the culmination of the efforts of a variety of commentators belonging to the tradition of Kallatar or “Kallata-acarya-paramparai.” A close study of the commentary reveals that the author must have studied the three commentaries of Ilampuranar, Cenavaraiyar and Naccinarkkiniyar with critical eyes and adopted only the best, in the various portions of his commentary.
The present edition is based on nine manuscripts, portions of Kallatar’s commentary printed in the issues of Nanacampantam and a few printed forms prepared by the late C.R.Namaccivaya Mutaliyar. Of the nine manuscripts, two manuscripts belong to our Library, one being D. 55 which is the original paper-manuscript and the other being R. 967 which is buta copy made from D. 55, with a portion of the commentary corrected on the basis of printed editions. A paper-manuscript which came into this Library, from Bavanantam Pillai’s Collections has also been used by the Editor. This Library also obtained a manuscript of this work from the Maturai-t-tamil-e-Cankam Library. All these have been listed by the Editor who had gone about collecting various manuscripts before he settled down to edit the work assigned to him. It is true more manuscripts must be in existence but are not unfortunately available. May I join the Editor in his appeal to such of those persons as may have manuscripts of this work in their possession to send them on to us, so that they may be used in the subsequent editions of this work? Need it be said that the help of such kindly people will be gratefully acknowledged by us?
In conclusion, I feel very much gratified to say that an excellent edition of Kallatar’s commentary of Tolkappiyam is now being placed before Tamil scholars, who, I am confident, will extend a warm welcome to it. It is my bounden duty to thank the Government of Madras which is always keen on promoting the cause of advanced knowledge which is the peerless glory of India, for having sanctioned the present publication at considerable cost. I must also express my profound gratitude to Professor T.P.Meenakshisundaranar, for having left no stone unturned in bringing out this valuable edition. I am also beholden to the Jamalia Press, Madras, (now defunct) and the Director of Stationery and Printing, Madras, for having finished the work assigned to them with expedition and efficiency.
R.K.PARTHASARATHI,
Formerly Curator and General Editor,
Madras Government Oriental Services
Government Oriental Manuscripts Library,
Madras-5

INTRODUCTION
I.
Tolkappiyam is the most ancient grammatical work in Tamil now available. There are some who will bring the age of Tolkappiyam down to the fifth century A.D. it is very difficult to date that work so definitely. But one can assign to it a chronology relative to the so called Cankam poetry. The fifth century is assigned to it by a few scholars on the ground they Tolkappiyam reveals post-Cankam usages and developments. But all those who have had intimate knowledge of the development of Tamil Language from Cankam age onwards, viz., the great commentators and the renowned grammarians of the modern age are confident that Tolkappiyar describes a pre-Cankam Tamil structure, such as the peculiar usage of [1] viyankol, [2] the particles of comparison, [3] the behavior of the roots Cel, Vam kotu, la, etc., and [4] the mentioning of names like Pen and such others, and [5] the rules with reference to most of the common words of every-day occurrences. These conflicting theories can be reconciled by taking the later-day feature found in Tolkappiyam as later additions. Scholars of the present day are more or less agreed on the last few cutras in Marapiyal being interpolations, because at least some of them are unnecessary repetitions.
One may, therefore, conclude that we have not received Tolkappiyam in the shape in which it was left by the author. Even Ilampuranar, the so-called earliest commentator, complains that certain cutras are missing (Tol-Porul-Cut Tiram 45). A detailed study will reveal many more losses and gains. The present writer has elsewhere pointed out the basic difference of approach between a majority of cutras in Tolkappiyam Eluttatikaram and a few particular cutras in the same work elsewhere.
The differences may be due to the rules belonging to different ages such as for instance, the rules relating to the viyankol and the rule relating to the use of the suffix kal. There may be interpolations by later-day copyists and students of Tolkappiyam making marginal notes for filling up lacunae, which they felt in the text, whenever they came to read or copy the book, centuries after the original was written.
If his is not accepted, the question may also be raised as to whether Tolkappiyam is the work of one author or is one like Manusmrti, etc., the work of a family or school of authors.
Eluthtikaram takes one view and Collatikaram another. But there are also differences even with reference to the theories relating to the same problemet for instance the basic form for the morpho-phonemic alternation of nivir and num. In spite of Cenavaraiyar’s attempted reconciliation, the difference remains much more fundamental than what he explains. These remarks are mainly intended as nothing more than suggestions for future research.
For the present, it is assumed on the basis of the major portion of this work that the cutras of Tolkappiyam belong to the pre-cankam period. The other points raised by scholars assigning a post-cankam period have been deal with in Camana-tamil Ilakkiya Varalaru by the present writer. After Tolkappiyam, perhaps another school opposing this grammar, probably, that of Avinayam grew, thanks to the Sanskrit influence; and finally succeeded more or less in driving Tolkappiyar into oblivion. There was somewhere at the end of the first millennium A.D. an attempt to revive the school of Tolkappiyar. Tolkappiyam is often mentioned even in cuttirams quoted with reverence in Yapparunkala virutti, though as far as prosody is concerned his views were not accepted evidently because Tamil versification has moved far away from his Age. Ilampuranar is hailed as the first commentator, probably because he was the earliest to write a commentary on Tolkappiyam. There must have been traces of older studies on Tolkappiyam as ia proved by his own remarks. Because of all these circumstances this commentator is not in a position to be definite in his statements in many places. He seems to be a great protagonist of the Tolkappiyam School even as Pallavataraiyar, as learnt from Mayilainatar’s commentary on Nannul, was a great protagonist of the Avinayam School. Gradually Hindu writers like Naccinarkkiniyar made a Risi of Tolkappiyar, as greatr as Vedic Risis.
II.
This work consists of three major divisions of (1) eluttatikaram which deals generally with what may be now called phonology, (2) Collatikaram which deals with what may be now called morphology and syntax and (3) Porulatikaram which generally deals with the subject matter of poetry, verses and poetics including prosody and poetic conventions. Of these Collatikaram has been studied by a number of authors because it gives what we may now describe as the grammatical structure.
We have a series of commentators on Collatikaram after Ilampuranar. In their hands every structure of Tamil becomes a Tolkappiyam usage. Though they feel that certain new changes have taken place even by the time of the Cankam Age, they try to bring them all within the four corners of Tolkappiyyam, though they are prepared to explain away later-day developments, as being due to the passage of time, a fact recognized by Tolkappiyar himself.  These commentaries are therefore valuable; for, without them for one thing, it will not be possible for us to understand the words and phrases used in Tolkappiyam. And for another, in the guise of interpretation, they write a new grammar of the Tamil of their own age. By careful study, one can distinguish in those commentaries, their own contributions and systematizations from the contributions of Tolkappiyar himself. In this way they form an important source for writing the History of the Tamil Language. The difference between Ilampuranar and Naccinarkkiniyar on Kurriyalukaram, for instance, occurring as utterance final and word final respectively, is really due to the respective ages in which they lived. After Ilampuranar came Cenavaraiyar; after him came peraciyar to be closely followed by Naccinarkkiniyar. Probably Kallatar came later as also Teyvaccilaiyar.
After these writers no one tried to write a commentary on Tolkappiyam. Instead, they introduced their researches on Tolkappiyam and is commentaries either in their own commentaries on Nannul, like Mayalainatar and Cankaranamaccivayar or in their own commentaries on their own works, like the authors of Ilakkana Vilakkam, Ilakkana k-kottu, Tonni and Muttuviriyam. Sivanana Swamikal in the guise of writing a commentary on the first cuttiram of Eluttatikaram has given us the results of his own researches. In the nineteenth century we have the editions of Tolkappiyam. Even in the ....th century scholars like Aracancannukanar, Aakkina-c-camikal, Maraimalai Atikal, P.S.Sastri, Mannarkuti Somasuntaram Pillai Venkata rajulu Reddiar and Venkatasami Nattar and others have only been translating writing commentaries, carrying on discussions or elucidating the problems of Tolkappiyam.
Today Tolkappiyam is in the hands of the students undergoing the Oriental and the Post-Graduate courses. Tolkappiyam Collatikaram with Cenacaraiyar’s commentary was published in the last century by Arumuka Navalar. Another edition was published by Srinivasa Raghava of the Presidency College. Ilampuranar’s commentary on Eludttu was published in this century by our patriot V.O. Chidambaram Pillai. His commentary on Porulatikaram was edited by Sri Vaiyapuri Pillai acting under the direction of V.O. Chidambaram Pillai, but unfortunately did not see the light of day. Sri Namaccivaya Mutaliyar brought out an edition of this work. Naccinarkkiniyar’s commentary on Collatikaram, as for those on other atikarams, was published by the late, lamented scholar and editor, Sri C.W.Damodaram Pillai in the nineteenth century itself. In this century Sri Venugopala Pillai published a new edition of Naccinarkkiniyar’s commentary on Collatikaram. Sri Ganesa Ayyar, the great scholar of Jaffna, unfortunately no more with us, has brought out a scholarly edition of Cenavaraiyar’s commentary with valuable foot notes. Kantaswamiyar, a scholar himself, trained under Aracanacanmukanar, brought out an edition published by South India Saiva Siddhantha Publishing Society, which has now issued a second edition with notes this time by Bhuvarakam Pillai. Karantai-t-tamil-c-Cankam which, under the leadership of Umamakeswaram Pillai, worked for Tamil culture and research, printed for the first time Teyvaccilaiyar’s commentary on Tolkappiyam. The commentary of Kallatar was printed in the pages of Nanacampantam by Sri Mahavidwan S.Dhandapani Desikar, but earlier to that attempt, Sri Namaccivaya Mutaliyar himself started printing Kallatdam in a book form but unfortunately it has not completed and only a few printed forms are available which were exhibited by the Saiva Siddhantha Publishing Society on the occasion of the release of their 1008th Publication.
III.
The advisory committee appointed by the Government of recommending Manuscripts to be printed by the Madras Government Oriental Manuscripts Library recommended the publication of Kallatar’s commentary on Tolkappiyam – Collatikaram. The present writer was requested to edit and a fair copy of the Manuscript was sent to him for being printed. He felt his function as an editor did not consist merely in sending to the press the Manuscript prepared by the copyists of the Library and certified to be correct. He therefore, went about collecting the Manuscripts. The Library itself got for him a copy of the Manuscript available in the Maturai-t-tamil-c-Cankam Library. Mr. S. Rajam of Murray and Company, arranged for the collection of this fair copy with the Cadjan leaf Manuscript containing the commentary of Kallatar, available in the Kalaksetra Library, Adyar, a manuscript belonging to Dr. Swaminatha Iyer. There was also another copy of the commentary which came into the Oriental Manuscripts Library from Bavanantam Pillai’s collections. Though Namaccivaya Mutaliyar’s edition was not available at that stage, Sri Venkatarajulu Reddiyar kindly gave his copy of that commentary. It was prepared from the Manuscripts on the basis of which Namaccivaya Mutaliyar’s edition was finalized. He also kindly gave the pages of the Magazine nanacampantam containing the Kallatar’s commentary. After the present edition was prepared and when the present writer came to serve in the Annamalai University, another copy of Kallatar’s commentary became available in the Annamalai University. The readings available in this last Manuscript could not be used in this edition. Therefore they are given later in this Introduction itself. There was also a copy of the commentary prepared by the present writer from the Manuscript copy available at the Manuscripts Library. The Manuscripts Library had an old Manuscript from which another copy was prepared and it is stated there in that a portion of the work had been corrected by comparison with the printed commentaries of Cenavaraiyar and Naccinarkkiniyar.
For convenience of reference these various manuscripts and printed works have been given Roman numbers in the foot notes where variant readings are noted. These are explained below.
[a] I is the original paper Manuscript in the Oriental Manuscripts Library. The paper used to look like newsprint and is already giving way crumbling to pieces in some places.
[b] II is a copy made from the above. But as already stated, the first portion has been corrected not on the basis of any other Manuscripts of Kallatar’s commentary but on the basis of printed editions of other commentaries. This manuscript is in a good condition of preservation. The hand-writing is much better than what is found in the first manuscript. It was this manuscript which was sent to the present writer duly copied and certified as correct, for being sent to the press. Simply because it was corrected by the copyist, it was not taken as the basis though the corrections in many places seem to be justified. But as faithfulness to the Manuscripts should be the first duty of the editor, the Present edition, has relied upon No. I. as the basic text. At the end of the commentary on sutram 254 this manuscript states that thereafter the rest is not available, though on the basis of No. I. it gives a few broken lines.
[c] III is the paper manuscript coming from the Bavanantam Academy. The second part of this volume is unfortunately not available and the first volume stops in the middle of the commentary on sutram 225. [With the end of the line 12 of pp. 300 of this edition; vide foot note 27 on that page.] The first volume is in a good condition of preservation and its handwriting is legible and good.
[d] IV is the Ms. Obtained from the Maturai-t-Tamil-c-cankam Library. It is a copy made from No. I and not from No. II. Therefore, this must have been prepared before No. II was prepared. But this is not a carefully-prepared copy. Many lines have been left out by the copyist. This is not helpful except in a few cases where portions in No. I have crumbled to pieces.
[e] V is the Ms. Copy prepared by Sri Venkatarajulu Reddiyar. It is written in the beautiful hand writing of Mr. Reddiyar himself. He has made his own corrections, but this corrections can be identified as may be seen from the foot notes to this edition. This stops with the end of the commentary on sutram 254. This agrees more with III.
[f] VI is the portion of Kallatar’s commentary printed in the issues of Nanacampantam by Sri. S.Dhandapani Desikar. This edition is prepared on the basis of No. II. But Sri. Desikar has made many alternations which are probably required for making any sense of the text in each places.
[g] VII is the Ms. Copied from No. II by the present writer, who had made his own corrections where ever he thought it necessary. This stops with sutram 254.
[h] VIII is the Ms. which belonged to Dr. Swaminata Iyer and which is now available at the KalakSetra Library. This has been responsible for restoring some of the portions not available in other copies. On the Ms. there is a statement that it is the copy of Satacivam Pillai if Jaffna, who was himself a disciple of Arumuka Navalar. This also stops with the commentary on sutram 254.
[i] IX is the Ms. copy available at the Annamalai University Library. But this is only the second volume of the commentary. It begins with the sutram 107 and its commentary. It is written on good paper which resists decay. As already stated this was not available when the present edition was printed and therefore this is utilized in an Appendix hereto. This Manuscripts stops with words “ariccollina –p-pinvarum collatu” on line 1 if page 344 of the present edition.
[j] X is the printed edition prepared by the late scholar C.R.Nanaccivaya Mutaliyar. But only a few formes are available.
[k] XI is the Ms. on whose basis the above X was prepared. This was also not available, but as already pointed out above, V was prepared from this XI.






The manuscripts may be arranged in the form of genealogical treed given above. Under the circumstances explained above it was not possible to reconstruct the manuscripts and then to proceed with the edition. This has therefore to be done at a later date.
These are all the editions and manuscripts which came to the knowledge of the present writer. It is unfortunate that other manuscripts which must be in existence have not been available. The present writer will be grateful to those who can send in the manuscripts of this work in their possession so that they can be used at least in the second edition, with their help being gratefully acknowledged.
The editor has not altered a single letter except on the authority of any original text. Naturally the texts like II, VI and VII could not be treated as basic texts for they have admittedly altered the texts as found in No. I and No. II. In the result only the readings in I, III and V and VIII have been studied for preparing the text and whenever one of the readings therein was found preferable, the editor has adopted it. If such help was not forthcoming from these basic texts he has left the reading as it is without trying to change it. But the footnote mentions all the readings, even those from altered texts and sometimes he has pointed out that such readings might have been the original. He has followed this policy even with reference to the quotations found from classics, without taking the liberty of correcting them on the basis of printed texts available from them in the hope that these variant readings even when not clear and sometimes full of mistakes may help scholars to find out for themselves the correct readings for the text quoted. He has in the footnotes pointed out the ambiguities possible in the scripts which may help to explain the different readings. A book on Paleography may make use of the explanations. It is unfortunate that it is not possible to give the Photostat copies of at least a few specimen leaves from these various manuscripts. The editor has not refrained from giving even what may appear to be absurd and impossible readings when they are so found. For one thing it is impossible to say that these may not be required at a later stage of research. We have only to note how readings rejected as absured being welcomed by recent researches on the texts of Shakespeare. For another, these mistakes even if they are such, throw some light on the pronunciation habits of the copyists and on the writing system of their day. This will be helpful to those who write the history of the colloquial Tamil language and the History of Tamil Writing System.
It is unfortunate that we know precious little about the commentator Kallatar. We have a poet of this name in the Cankam Age. There is another as the author of Kannappar Maram found included in the eleventh Saiva Tirumurai. There is also the author of the poetic work Kallatam, who probably, came a little later. The author of our commentary is probably the fourth person of the same name of literary eminence. Kallatam is referred to as the name of a place by Manikkavacakar in his Tiruvacakam. Perhaps, this name has therefore nothing to do with Kallata, found as the name of a great writer in Kashmi Saivim.
Kallatar’s commentary is referred to only by Subramanya Dhiksitar in his own commentary on his Tamil Grammatical Work, Pirayoka Vivekam. He belongs to the early years of the eighteenth century. That fixes only the lower limit for the age of this commentary Naccinarkkiniyar who refers by name to Cenavaraiyar and Ilampuranar deos not mention Kallatar. But it must be said he does not mention the name of every one whose theory he criticizes or refers to. But the commentary, as it stands today, is full of passages from Ilampuranar, Cenavaraiyar and Naccinarkkiniyar and in many places the language of the commentary cannot be on any grounds be assigned to any age older than Naccinarkkiniyar’s.
There is however, one important statement to be made. As one knows very well from the commentary on Iraiyanar Akapporul, a commentary can be handed over as a tradition by word of mouth from teacher to student for many generations. In such a case one cannot say that the particular words in which the interpretation is written down at a later date where the very words used by the earliest authors. Here one can be sure of only the general idea; but even there, there is no guarantee that they are really ancient; for, the tradition naturally makes subsequent improvements elaborations and alternations even with reference to these general ideas. In such a case one is justified in taking the written commentary when available as the culmination of the tradition of a school rather than of any single author. In this sense, it may be possible to assign this commentary to one such tradition, specially characterized by the thoughts referred to as Kallatar’s in Pirayoka Vivekam, whereas in other cases the teachers of that tradition must have accepted the ideas of other commentators according to their own likes, and dislikes, sometimes accepting the view of one commentator and at other times, the view of other commentators, who all necessarily must have become famous by the time of this commentary came to be reduced writing in the sixteenth or seventeenth Century after Naccinarkkiniyar. Naccinarkkiniyar thus will fix the upper limit for this written form of commentary, though not for the tradition of this commentary itself.
The first sutram has an elaborate commentary following probably the style of the commentary on Iraiyanar Akapporul. How far this goes back to the original tradition one cannot now assert. The various copies of the commentary itself seem to show that much emphasis was not placed on copying or preserving the exact words. One has only to refer to the beginning portions of every one of these sutras which goon varying.

This is one way of writing. On this basis one should not argue that Kallatar is the ciuaháÇa® referred to by Cenavaraiyar. The real explanation probably is that in the present available edition of Ilampuranar the particular passages are missing whilst they were there when Kallatar took those passages from the Ilampuranar’s commentary available to him.

cū. 1
vaḻuvamaiyāc colliṟkē ilakkaṇam kūṟavēṇṭum.ēḻu vaḻuvakuttuk kūṟiṉāl vaḻu vilakkaṇam evvāṟu vaḻuvamaiyāc colliṟku ilakkaṇamātal kūṭum? vaḻuppōla varutalākātu eṉa iṉmai mukattāl allatu etir maṟaimukattāl kūṟiyatāka ivvaḻu vārāycciyaik koḷḷutal vēṇṭum eṉpatu inta uraippakutiyiṉ poruḷākum
eṉṉutal eṉṟu kūṟuka.
cū. 1
eḻuttōṭu puṇarntu poruḷaṟivikkum ōcai ilakkaṇattil ārāyappaṭuvatutāṉ iyalpu. eḻuttōṭu puṇarntē (puṇarntiruntum) poruḷai valiyuṟuttātu varuvaṉavaṟṟaiyum ivvatikāram ārāyvatākak kūṟuvatu aiyattai viḷaivikkiṉṟatu. ivaṟṟai vaḻuveṉak kāṭṭi ārāykiṉṟatu pōlum.
cū. 1
ivvōttu eṉṉa peyarttō eṉiṉ - itaṉai “ivvōttu eṉṉa peyarttō?” eṉṉa - eṉpatākak koḷka. inta uraiyil ivvāṟu kēḷvik kuṟiyōṭu muṭiyum coṟṟoṭarkaḷai “	” ikkuṟiyīṭukaḷiṉ iṭaiyē varuvaṉa vākak koḷka. 6-17.
cū. 1
piṟa ottukkaḷil (iyalkaḷil) coṟkaḷ poruḷkaḷ mēlāyum niṉṟapōtu avaṟṟiṉ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷai uṇarttiṉār. āṉāl coṟkaḷ poruḷkaḷ mēlāmāṟṟaiyē inta iyal viḷakkukiṟatu.
8-ep. 1.65.111. vakaiya lallatillai eṉṟirukkumāṉāl iraṇṭu lakarattiṟkumēl mūṉṟu lakaram eṇṇikkaiyil tavaṟi eḻutiviṭṭatākak koṇṭāl ippatippil koṇṭa pāṭamum ataṉāl ottuppōkum. itaṉaiyē aṭikkuṟippu viḷakkukiṟatu.
cū. 1
eṉṟār eṉṟaṟpālataṉai - iṅku ṉakarattiṉ mēṟpuḷḷi paḻaiya ēṭukaḷil iruppataṟkillai. eṉavē ataṉai uyir meyyākak koḷḷalām. aṭuttu ṟakarattiṟkup patilāka iraṇṭiṉait tavaṟāka eḻutiyatākak koḷḷalām. appōtu “eṉṟār eṉaṟpālataṉai” āṉāl kiṭaitta pāṭaṅkaḷi lellām acciṭṭapaṭiyēyuḷḷamaiyāl avvāṟē patippikka vēṇṭiyatāyiṟṟu.
cū. 1
oru cūttirattil pala coṟṟoṭarkaḷ varalām. appōtu poruṭpaṭai allatu poruṭpaṭuttuvatu eṉṟu kūṟakkūṭiya viṉaiccol coṟṟoṭaril īṟṟil varum. avvāṟu oru vākkiyam muṭintapiṉ maṟṟōr vākkiyam varumāṉāl mutal vākkiyattiṉ īṟṟuc colliṟkum, iraṇṭām vākkiyattiṉ mutaṟcolliṟkum poruḷ toṭarpō aḵtāvatu poruḷiyaipō allatu colliyaipō iruppataṟkillai. ikkālattil itaṉai araippuḷḷi iṭṭu viḷakkuvōm; muḻup puḷḷiyiṭṭāl cūttiram oṉṟāmāṟillai.
cū. 4
ivveṉa-iv eṉpataṟku ivaiyeṉṟē poruḷ. 13-ep. eṉa 1.98 kūṟiyatupōl iṅkuk koḷḷalākātu.
cū. 7
ivvari iṅku utāraṇamāmāṟu illai. “ārttār koṇṭatu vantār” eṉpatōṭu cērntuvanta ceyyuṭpakuti ākalāṉ.

kīḻkkuṟippil koṇṭa pāṭam poruntalām.

cū. 17
cōḻanāṭu, arumaruntaṉṉāṉ mutaliya pōla ilakkaṇa “vāypāṭu amainta vaḻakkukaḷum uḷvaḻi arumaruntāṉa, cōṇāṭu eṉac coṟ citaiyavum varuvaṉa ilakkaṇattoṭu poruntā marūu vaḻakku allatu marūu vaḻakku eṉappaṭum.
cū. 25
piṉṉum viṉaiyiyaluḷ aṉmai iṉmai eṉpavaṟṟaip paṇpup peyarākavē koḷvar. 263-ām pārkka.
cū. 32
ainterutu uṭaiyavaṉoruvaṉ taṉ erutukaḷai avaṟṟaik kāppavaṉiṭattil koṭuppa, piṉṉar avaṟṟil ōrerutu kāṇāmaṟ pōykkeṭṭatu. ataṉaik kūṟavanta kāppāṉ “oṉṟu keṭṭatu” eṉṟu uṭaiyavaṉiṭattuc colliṉāṉ. avvāṟu colliṉatai yaṭuttu erutiṉaiuṭaiyāṉ “veḷḷaiyerutō kārerutō keṭṭatu” eṉṉum aiyantīrtaṟporuṭṭu “avaṟṟuḷ yātu” eṉṟu viṉāvutalē iṅkuk kuṟikkappaṭuvatu. naṭu eṉpatu 5-āvatu variyil naṭu eṉa uḷḷatu viḷaṅkavillai; iṭaiyeṉṟu itaṉait tirittum payaṉillai. colluvatu uṭaiyāṉiṭaiyēyaṉṟik kāppāṉiṭaiyēyaṉṟu. ellāp piratikaḷilum iṅkē eḻuttukkaḷ iruttalāl 5-ām piratiyiṉ pāṭattaik koḷvataṟkillai. “kēṭu” eṉṟu irukkalām.
cū. 41
koḷḷappaṭum eṉpataṉaik koḷḷutal vēṇṭum eṉṟu poruḷ koḷka. illaiyāṉāl avaṟṟai eṉpatu tavaṟum. itu tirukkuṟaḷilum varum vaḻakku.
cū. 58
eṉṉaieṉa varum iraṇṭiloṉṟu mikai pōlum.
cū. 58
intac cūttirattil varum kālam mutaliyaṉavum, muṉ cūttirattil varum kuṭimai mutaliyaṉavum uyartiṇaip poruḷil vantum aḵṟiṇai muṭipu koḷḷukiṉṟaṉavātaliṉ iraṇṭaiyumoṉṟākki oru cūttiramāka ōtātatu eṉṉai eṉak kēḷvi eḻukiṟatu. kuṭimai mutaliyaṉavellām paṇpaṭiyāṉ niṉṟu paṇpuṭaiya poruḷaik kuṟittu apporuḷ mēl nikaḻkiṉṟaṉa (varukiṉṟaṉa). ātaliṉ avaṟṟai muṉṉattiṉuṇaruṅ kiḷavieṉa oruvakaiyākki avvāṟu muṉṉattiṉuṇarap paṭuvaṉavākak kiḷavātu varum kālum mutaliya peyarkaḷai vēṟōr vakaiyākki āciriyar kūṟiṉār eṉpatu akkēḷvikku viṭai. oṉpatāvatu variyil oruvayaiyāṟa kiḷavātu varum eṉṟa pakuti viḷaṅkavillai. “oruvakaiyākkik kiḷavātu varum” eṉṟu pāṭam koḷḷavēṇṭum pōlum.

cū. 227
“ciṉaiviṉai mutalviṉaiyoṭu” eṉpatupaṟṟiyē iṅku eḻuntatu ārāycci. avvāṟāṉāl ōr aiyam eḻukiṟatu. “mutala (cāttaṉ) eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaiyāka vārāmal vēṟu vakaiyāka varuvatum uṇṭu. cāttaṉatu kaiyiṟṟu vīḻntatu eṉa varum viṉaimuṭipu ic cūttirattiṉ vitikoṇṭu “cāttaṉatu kaiyiṟṟu vīḻntāṉ” eṉak koḷḷavum kūṭumaṉṟō” eṉpatē iṅkeḻum kēḷvi. avvāṟu varutalākātu eṉpatu umairayāciriyar kūṟum muṭipu. ciṉai mutaloṭu muṭital. ic cūttirattiṉ vitikoṇṭu “cāttaṉatu kaiyiṟṟu vīḻntāṉ” eṉak koḷḷavum kūṭumaṉṟō eṉpatē iṅkeḻum kēḷvi. avvāṟu varutalākātu eṉpatu uraiyāciriyar kūṟum muṭivuṣapu. ciṉai mutaloṭu muṭital iccūttirattil kūṟappeṟṟuḷḷatu; atu aṅṅaṉam nikaḻvatu mutal eḻuvāyākip payaṉilaiyām muṭivukoḷḷum vaḻiyē eṉa ivvuraiyāciriyar viḷakkukiṟār.

VIII
It is with great pleasure that I acknowledge my indebtedness to the Advisoryt Committee who entrusted me with this work, to the successive Curators who rendered all the help necessary, to Sri S. Rajam who made it possible for meto collate tghe present edition with the valuable manuscript of Dr. Swaminata Aiyar, to the authorities of Dr. Swaminata Aiyar’s Library for allowing this study of collation through my assistant, Sri Venkatarajalu Reddiar who so kindly lent me his manuscript copy and his copy of Nanacampantam, to Sri K.Arumugam, M.A., M.Litt., Reader in Tamil, Delhi University, who pointed out the mistakes in the printed text, to Sri P. Arunachalam, Sri E. Annamalai, M.A., Lectyurers, Tamil (Arts) Department and Sri G. Vijayavenugopal, M.A., for preparing the concordance of Collatikaram commentaries, to Kumari A. Parameswari and Kumari N. Rajalakshmi, the University Grants Commission Research Scholars for preparing the readings available from the Annamalai University Manuscript, and lastly to the authorities of Annamalai University for permitting to use their valuable manuscript. Nothing in this world is an unmixed happiness. I am very sorry that my good friend Sri Sivaprakasam Pillai, the copyist of the Government of Madras Oriental Manuscript Library who so willingly co-operated with me in collating all the manuscripts and copies, in preparing the copy for print and in correcting, proofs, is not now alive to see this book in its final form. Sri Venkatarajulu Reddiar also in no more with us. I owe deep debt of gratitude to them and I only hope their souls will rest in peace and bless this edition now that the book has been placed before the scholarly public.
T. P. MEENAKSHISUNDARAM.



kallāṭaṉār virutti (vēṟu urai)
am: mūṉṟu kūṟṟuc collum uyartiṇaiyai aṟivikkum collām.
avvē eṉa āṟāvataṉ paṉmaiyākak koḷka.      (2)
3. 	oṉṟaṟi collē palavaṟi colleṉ
	ṟāyiru pāṟ co laḵṟiṇai yavvē.
e-tu āṇ peṇṇākiya oṉṟaṉaiyum avviraṇṭiṉatu paṉmaiyākiya palavaṟṟaiyum aṟivikkum colloṭu poruntiya avviraṇṭu kūṟṟuc collum aḵṟiṇaiyai aṟivikkum collām.
uyartiṇaiyavvē, aḵṟiṇaiyavvē e-tu, uyartiṇai eṉa am muppāṟ col aḵṟiṇai eṉa āyirupāṟcol eṉa.
ē īṟṟacai.
4.	peṇmai cuṭṭiya uyartiṇai maruṅki
	ṉāṇmai tirinta peyarnilaik kiḷaviyun
	teyvañ cuṭṭiya peyarnilaik kiḷaviyu
	mivveṉa vaṟiyu mantan tamakkilavē
	uyartiṇai maruṅkiṟ pālpiṟin ticaikkum.
e - tu peṇmait taṉmai eytiya uyartiṇaiyiṭattu āṇmai nīṅkiya peyarp poruḷum teyvañ cuṭṭiya peyarp poruḷum pālaṟiya niṟkum īṟṟeḻuttu it taṉmaiyaṉa eṉat tamakku uṭaiyavalla; uyartiṇai muppāliṉaiyum viḷakkum eḻuttiṉaiyē īṟāka icaikkum.
‘u - m : - pēṭi vantāṉ, pēṭi vantāḷ, pēṭiyar vantār.
vācutēvaṉ vantāṉ, tiruviṉāḷ vantāḷ, muppattu mūvarum vantār eṉavarum. uyartiṇai maruṅkiṉ eṉpataṉai āṇmai cuṭṭiya eṉṟu moḻimāṟṟi uraikka. antan tamakkilavē eṉṟataṉāṟ nākaṉ vantāṉ, nāki vantāḷ, nākar vantār eṉak tēvarum makkaḷum allāta nākarkkum am muṭivu koḷka.
5.	ṉaḵkā ṉoṟṟē yāṭūu vaṟicol.
e - tu, ṉakara voṟṟē āṇmakaṉai aṟivikkum col.
u - m : - uṇṭāṉ. uṇṇā niṉṟāṉ. uṇpāṉ eṉa mūṉṟu kāla viṉaiyāṉum, kariyaṉ ceyyaṉ eṉak kālan tōṉṟāta viṉaik kuṟippāṉum aṟika.
6.	ḷaḵkā ṉoṟṟē makaṭūu vaṟicol.
e - tu.
u - m : - uṇṭāḷ, uṇṇā niṉṟāḷ, uṇpāḷ eṉṉum mūṉṟu kāla viṉaiyāṉum, kariyaḷ, ceyyuḷ eṉṉum viṉaikkuṟippāṉum uṇarka.
7.	raḵkā ṉoṟṟum pakara viṟutiyum
	māraik kiḷavi yuṭpaṭa mūṉṟum
	nērat tōṉṟum palaraṟi collē.
u - m : - uṇṭār, uṇṇā niṉṟār, uṇpār eṉṉum mūṉṟu kāla viṉaiyāṉum; kariyār, ceyyār eṉṉum viṉaik kuṟippāṉum uṇpa, tiṉpa, koṇmār vantār eṉa etirkāla muṟṟuc collāṉum aṟika.
8.	oṉṟaṟi kiḷavi taṟaṭa vūrnta
	kuṟṟiya lukarat tiṟatiyākum
u - m : - uṇṭatu, uṇṇā niṉṟatu, uṇpatu; karitu, ceytu eṉak kālam mūṉṟāṉum, viṉaik kuṟippāṉum, kūyiṟṟu; tāyiṟṟu; kōṭiṉṟu; kuḷampiṉṟu; ukiriṉṟu; mayiriṉṟu eṉa iṟanta kālattāṉum viṉaik kuṟippāṉum, kuṇṭukaṭṭu; koṭuntāṭṭu eṉṟa viṉaikkuṟippāṉum taṟaṭakkaḷai ūrntuvanta kuṟṟiyalukattāṉ oṉṟaṟicol eṉṟu aṟika.
9.	a ā va veṉa varūu miṟuti
	yappāṉ mūṉṟē palavaṟi collē.
u - m : - uṇṭaṉa; uṇṇā niṉṟaṉa; uṇpaṉa; kariya; ceyya eṉak kālaṅkaḷāṉum, viṉaikkuṟippāṉum uṇṇā; tiṉṉā eṉṉum muṟṟuc collāṉum uṇkuva; tiṉkuva eṉa etirkālattāṉum āka a ā va eṉṉum am mūṉṟu collāṉum paṉmaiyāṉum uṇarappaṭum eṉṟu aṟika.
10.	irutiṇai maruṅki ṉaimpā laṟiya
	vīṟṟuniṉ ṟicaikkum patiṉō reḻuttun
	tōṟṟan tāmē viṉaiyoṭu varumē.
u - m : uyartiṇaiyiṭattum aḵṟiṇaiyiṭattum oruvaṉ, orutti, palar, oṉṟu, pala eṉṉum aintu pālum aṟiya iṟutiyiṉiṉṟuṇarttum patiṉōreḻuttum pulappaṭu miṭattu viṉaiyoṭu naṭakkum. avaiyāvaṉa : - ṉakāramum ḷakāramum rakāramum pakāramumākum. tavvum ṟavvum ṭavvum avvum āvum vavvum eṉa ivai muṉcoṉṉa cūttiraṅkaḷiṉ viṉaiyoṭu naṭakkumāṟu kaṇṭukoḷka.
11.	viṉaiyiṟ ṟōṉṟum pālaṟi kiḷaviyum
	peyariṟ ṟōṉṟum pālaṟi kiḷaviyu
	mayaṅkal kūṭāta tammara piṉavē.
e - tu. viṉaiyiṉāṟ pālaṟiyappaṭum poruḷum, peyariṉāṟ pālaṟiyappaṭum poruḷum mayaṅkutalaip poruntā. avai tattam ilakkaṇattaṉavāyē varum.
u - m : - uṇṭāṉ avaṉ, uṇṭāḷ avaḷ, uṇṭār avar, uṇṭatu atu, uṇṭaṉa avai eṉa viṉai niṟpa peyarmēl tattam marapiṉāṉ vantaṉa.
avaṉ uṇṭāṉ, avaḷ uṇṭāḷ, avar uṇṭār, atu uṇṭatu, avai uṇṭaṉa eṉa ivaip peyar niṟpa viṉaimēl tattam marapiṉāṉ vantaṉa.
iṉi mayaṅkal kūṭā eṉavē avaṟṟu mayakkam uṇṭeṉ patu collappaṭṭatām. am mayakkam eḻuvakaippaṭum.
avai āvaṉa :- tiṇai mayaṅkivantuḻi vaḻuveṉṟu koḷka (kaka)
12.	āṇmai tirinta peyarnilaik kiḷavi
	yāṇmai yaṟicoṟ kākiṭa ṉiṉṟē
emmūr aracaṉ nallaṉō ummūrk kōlikaṉ nallaṉō eṉṟum ciṉaiyu mutalum piṟaḻap porūuvaṟka.
uppakka nōkki ṉupatēci tōṇmaṇantā
ṉuttara māmaturaik kacceṉpa - vippakka
mātāṉa paṅki maṟuvil pukaḻc cennāp
pōtār puṉaṟ kūṭaṟ kaccu.
mātāṉapaṅkiyāvār tiruvaḷḷuva tēvar.
naṟunīla neytaluṅ koṭṭiyun tīṇṭip
piṟanāṭṭup peṇṭir muṭināṟum pāri
paṟanāṭṭup peṇṭi raṭi.
mēṟṟā ṉirunta vuyarciṉaik koṉṟāṉiṟ
ṟēṟṟā voḻukkam paṭuvaṉakoṉ - māṟṟār
uṟumuraṇ cāyttā ṉuyarkuṭantai yāṭṭic
ciṟu maruṅkul ceṟṟa mulai.
eṉṉum pāṭṭukkaḷ mutalum ciṉaiyum piṟaḻntaṉa vāyiṉum piṟaḻvalla eṉṟu ettuṇaiyaṉa porūuka.
in naṅkai kaṇ nallavō, kayal nallavō eṉavum, pulipōlap pāyntāṉ eṉavum, maḻai vaṇkai eṉavum, tuṭipōl naṭu eṉavum, vēypōlun tōḷ eṉavum, poṉpōlu mēṉi muttuppōlum pal eṉavum vantaṉavāl eṉiṉ aṉṉavai piṟaḻvalla. eṉṉai?
viṉaipaya meyyuru veṉṟa nāṉkē
vakaipeṟa vanta vuvamat tōṟṟam eṉpataṉāluṟaḻka.
apporuḷākum eṉṟataṉāl eṇṇumiṭattu muttum pavaḷamum maṇiyum poṉṉum eṉṟeṇṇuka. muttuṅ karuviḷam paralu maṇiyuṅ kāṉaṅ kōḻiyum poṉṉum eṉṟu eṇṇaṟka.	(kacu)
17.	takutiyum vaḻakkun taḻīiyiṉa voḻukum
	pakutik kiḷavi varainilai yilavē.
e - tu : takuti paṟṟiyum vaḻakkup paṟṟiyum porunti naṭakkum ilakkaṇap pakutic col kaṭiyappaṭā.
takuti iraṇṭu vakaippaṭum : - maṅkala marapiṉāṟ kūṟutalum iṭakkar aṭakkik kūṟutalum eṉa.
cettārait tuñciṉār eṉṟalum, cuṭukāṭṭai naṉkāṭu eṉṟalum, ōlaiyait tirumukam eṉṟalum, viḷakkup perukiṟṟu eṉṟalum ittoṭakkattaṉa maṅkala marapu.
kaṇkaḻīi varutum, kāṉmēl nīr peytuvarutum, antitoḻutuvarutum, kaikkuṟiyarāyiruntār, poṟai yuyirttār eṉṉun toṭakkattaṉa iṭakkaraṭakku.
iṉi vaḻakkāvatu nāṉku vakaippaṭum. avai, ilakkaṇa vaḻakku, ilakkaṇattōṭu poruntiya marūvuvaḻakku, marūvuvaḻakku, kuḻuviṉ - vanta kuṟunila vaḻakku eṉpavaṟṟuḷ nilam, nīr, tī, vaḷi, ākāyam, cōṟu, kūḻ, pāl, pāvitam, makkaḷ, maram, teṅku, kamuku ittoṭakkattaṉa ilakkaṇa vaḻakku.
iṉmuṉ eṉpataṉai muṉṟil eṉṟum, kaṇmī eṉpataṉai mīkaṇ eṉṟum, yāvar eṉpataṉai yār eṉṟum varutal ilakkaṇattoṭu poruntiya varūuvaḻakku.
arumaruntaṉṉāṉ eṉpataṉai arumaruntāṉ eṉṟum, potuvil eṉpataṉaip potiyil eṉṟum, malaiyamāṉāṭu eṉpataṉai malāṭu eṉṟum, cōḻaṉāṭu eṉpataṉaic cōṇāṭu eṉṟum collutaṉ marūu vaḻakku.
poṟkollar poṉṉaip paṟi eṉṟalum, vaṇṇakkar kāraṇattai nīlam eṉṟalum, yāṉaippākar āṭaiyaik kūṟai eṉṟalum, iḻiciṉar cōṟṟaic coṉṟi eṉṟalum kuḻuviṉ vanta kuṟunila vaḻakku.
iṉi oru cārār kuḻuviṉvanta kuṟunilavaḻakkum, iṭakkaraṭakkalum maṅkala marapiṉāṟ kūṟutalum eṉṟa im mūṉṟaiyum takkavāṟu collutalāṉ takuti eṉṟākki, oḻintavaṟṟaiyellām vaḻakkāṟu eṉpa.
iṉi, avar vaḻakkāṟu collumpaṭi ; karitu veḷitu eṉ kaiyum, kiḻakku mēṟku eṉkaiyum, ciṟitu periyatu eṉkaiyum, karumai cemmaiyākiya paṇpiṉālaṉṟi veḷḷāḷarai veṇkaḷamar eṉṟum pulaiyaraik karuṅkaḷamar eṉṟum cātipaṟṟi veḷḷāṭu eṉṟum collutal vaḻuvaṉṟu; cevvaḻakku eṉpa, taḻīiyaṉa oḻukum eṉṟamaiyāl eṉpa.
18.	iṉaccuṭ ṭillāp paṇpukoḷ peyarkkoṭai
	vaḻakkā ṟalla ceyyu ḷāṟē.
e - tu. iṉattaic cuṭṭātu paṇpu koṇṭu niṉṟa peyarc coṟkaḷ vaḻakku neṟiyalla, ceyyuḷ neṟiyām.
u - m: - ceññāyiṟṟuk kaviṉa, veṇkōṭṭi yāṉai, neṭuveṇ tiṅkaḷ, veṇmuttam eṉac ceyyuḷuḷ varum.
iṉik kuṟuñcūli, neṭuntakaṭi, kuṟumūkki, kuṟumpūḻ eṉpaṉavum iṉañcuṭṭātu vaḻakkiṉil vantaṉavāl eṉiṉ, avai avaṟṟiṉ pērē; iṉañcuṭṭatu vantaṉa alla eṉṟu maṟukka.
19.	iyaṟkaip poruḷai yiṟṟeṉak kiḷattal.
e - tu. ulakattiyalpāki vārāniṉṟa poruḷkaḷaic collumiṭattittaṉmai yeṉṟē colluka.
u - m : - nilam valitu, nīr taṇṇitu, tī veytu, vaḷi uḷarum, uyir uṇarum eṉa ivvāṟu colluvatu.
iṉi, nilam valitāyiṟṟu, nīr taṇṇitāyiṟṟu eṉṟu collaṟka. maṟṟu nilam valitāyiṟṟu eṉṟum collupavāl eṉiṉ, aḵtu iyalpaṉṟu. cēṟṟunilam mitittu vaṉṉilam mitikkaiyāl vikāram eṉpatu.
20. 	ceyaṟkaip poruḷai yākkamoṭu kūṟal.
e - tu. ceyaṟkaiyākiya poruḷkaḷai ellām ākkaṅ koṭuttuc colluka.
u - m:- mayir nalla āyiṉa, paiṅkūḻ nalla āyiṉa eṉac colluka. āyiṉa eṉpatu ākkam.
21.	ākkan tāṉē kāraṇa mutaṟṟē.
e - tu. av ākkantaṉṉaic collumiṭattuk kāraṇa muṉ vaittu ākkattaip piṉ vaittuc colluka.
u - m :- kaṭuvuṅ kaipiḻi eṇṇeyum peṟṟamaiyāṉ mayir nalla āyiṉa eṉavum, eruppeytu iḷaṅkaḷaikaṭṭu nīrkālyāttamaiyāṟpaiṅkūḻ nalla āyiṉa eṉavum colluka. kaṭuvuṅ kaipiḻi eṇṇeyum kāraṇam; mayir nalla āyiṉatu eṉpa ākkam.	(uka)
collumāṟu : - peṇṭāṭṭi allaḷ āṇmakaṉ eṉṟum, āṇmakaṉ allaṉ peṇṭāṭṭi eṉṟum, oṉṟaṉṟu pala eṉṟum, pala alla oṉṟu eṉṟum collupa. avar coṉmuṟai aṟiyār; ātalāṉ aḵtu uraiyaṉmai aṟika. itu ammuṉṟiṭattum tuṇivu tōṉṟiyavaḻic coṉṉikaḻvu māṟu kūṟiṟṟu.		(uru)
26.	aṭaiciṉai mutaleṉa muṟaimūṉṟu mayaṅkāmai
	naṭaipeṟ ṟiyalum vaṇṇac ciṉaiccol.
e - tu. vaṇṇac ciṉaic collātu aṭaiyum ciṉaiyum mutalum mūṉṟum aṭaṅkāmai aṭaivē vaḻakkup peṟṟu naṭakkum.
aṭaiyeṉpatoru poruḷatu kuṇam; ciṉai uṟuppu; mutal avviraṇṭaiyum uṭaiya poruḷ.
u - m : peruntalaic cāttaṉ, peruntōṇ maṇṇai, neṭṭilait teṅku, ceṅkaṇ varāl, ceṅkāṉārai eṉa varum.
aṭaiciṉai mutaleṉa mūṉṟum eṉṉātu muṟai eṉṟataṉāṉ am muṟaiyiṉṟi naṭattalum uṭaiya. avai vaḻakkiṉum kuṇam - iraṇṭaṭukki mutaloṭu varutalum ceyyuḷuḷ kuṇam iraṇṭaṭukkic ciṉaiyoṭu varutalum eṉa;
avai varumāṟu:-
iḷam peruṅ kūṟṟaṉ; ciṟukaruñ cāttaṉ itu vaḻakku
ciṟupaintūvi, ceṅkāṟ pēṭai itu ceyyuḷ, piṟavum aṉṉa.
mutaloṭu kuṇam iraṇṭaṭukkutal vaḻakkiyal.
ciṉaiyōṭaṭukkutal ceyyuḷāṟē.        
 itu piṟa cūttiram.
ataṉāṉ av ilēcu koḷka.
27.	oruvaraik kūṟum paṉmaik kiḷaviyum
	oṉṟaṉaik kūṟum paṉmaik kiḷaviyum
	vaḻakki ṉākiya uyarcoṟ kiḷavi
	yilakkaṇa maruṅkiṟ collā ṟalla.
e - tu. uyartiṇai oruvaraip paṉmaiyāṟ collutalum aḵṟiṇai oṉṟaṉaip paṉmaiyāṟ collutalum eṉa ivai vaḻakkiṉākiṉṟakattu uyarttuc collappaṭum; ,lakkaṇa muṟaimaiyāṟ collumiṭattu neṟiyalla.
u - m :- oruvaṉaiyum tām vantār eṉpa. oruttiyaiyum tām vantār eṉpa. oṉṟaṉaiyum tām vantār eṉpa. itu vaḻuvē. āyiṉum amaika.
aṉṟiyum yām vantēm, nīyir vantīr, ivar vantār eṉa oruvaṉaiyum oṉṟaṉaiyum uyarttic colliyavāṟu tām vantār toṇṭaṉār eṉpatu uyarcol kuṟippu nilaiyā yiṉ, iḻipu viḷakkiṟṟu.
ilakkaṇa maruṅkiṟ collāṟalla eṉṟataṉāl uyir piṉum iḻipiṉum uvappiṉum ciṟappiṉum uyartiṇaiyai? aḵṟiṇaiyē pōlac collutalum aḵṟiṇaiyai uyartiṇaiyē pōlac collutalum vaḻakkiṉuḷ varum.
kuricil vantatu eṉpatu uyarpu poṟiyili vantatu iḻipu. oruvaṉai eṉ yāṉai vantatu eṉṟum, oruttiyai eṉ pāvai vantatu eṉṟum, ōreruttiṉai eṉ entai vantār eṉṟum, oru pacuviṉai eṉ aṉṉai vantāḷ eṉṟum collutal uvappu. cāttaṉāl vantār, nariyiṉār vantār eṉal ciṟappu. ivaṟṟil tiṇai pāl mayakkam kāṇka. 	(ue)
28	celaviṉum varaviṉum taraviṉum koṭaiyiṉum
	nilaipeṟan tōṉṟum annāṟ collum
	taṉmai muṉṉilaip paṭarkkai yeṉṉu
	mammū viṭattu muriya veṉpa.
e - tu. cellum varum tarum koṭukkum eṉa nilai peṟṟup pulappaṭṭu niṉṟa annāṉku collum taṉmai muṉṉilai paṭarkkai eṉṉum ammūṉ ṟiṭattiṟkum uriya.
u - m :- muṉṉark kāṭṭutum.
29.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	tarucol varucol āyiru kiḷaviyum
	taṉmai muṉṉilai yāyī riṭatta.
e - tu. u - m: - eṉakkut taruṅ kāṇam, eṉakkuvaruṅ kāṇam, niṉakkuvarum kāṇam eṉa varum.
30.	ēṉai yiraṇṭu mēṉai yiṭatta.
e - tu. cellum koṭukkum eṉpaṉa paṭarkkaiyiṭattaṉa.
u - m :- avarkkuc celluṅ kāṇam. avarkkuk koṭukkuṅ kāṇam eṉa varum.
annāṟ collum taṉmai - muṉṉilai paṭarkkai - eṉṉum ammūviṭattum uriya veṉpa eṉṟataṉāṉ avai mayaṅkivaravum peṟum.
“tūṇṭil vēṭṭuvaṉ vāṅka vārātu
  puṉaṟaru pacuṅkāy tiṉṉataṉ ṟappal”      - eṉavum,
“arimala rāyntakaṇ ṇammā kaṭaici
  tirumukamun tiṅkaḷuñ cettut - terumantu
  vaiyattum vāṉattuñ cellā taṇaṅkāki
  yaiyattu niṉṟa tarā.”
eṉavum mayaṅkuvaṉa amaikka. kaṭaici! eṉa muṉṉilai ākkuka.
31.	yāteva ṉeṉṉu māyiru kiḷaviyum
	aṟiyāp poruḷvayiṟ ceṟiyat tōṉṟum.
e - tu. yātu evaṉ eṉṉum iraṇṭu collum taṉṉāl aṟiyappaṭāta poruṭkaṇ viṉāvāyt tōṉṟum.
u - m :- nunnāṭi yātu? ippaṇṭiyuḷḷatu evaṉ? maṭiyuḷḷatu yātu? maṭiyuḷḷatu evaṉ? kaiyuḷḷatu yātu? kaiyuḷḷatu evaṉ? eṉavarum. iccoṟkup poruḷ yātu iccoṟkup poruḷ evaṉ eṉavum, evaṉ eṉpatu ikkālattuḷ eṉ eṉṟum eṉṉaiyeṉṟum tirintu naṭakkum. ceṟiya eṉṟataṉāl yāvaṉ. yāvaṉ, yāvar, yātu, yāvai eṉavum, yāṅku, yāṇṭu, epporuḷ eṉa ivaiyum tiṇaipāl mutaliyaṉavum aṟiyappaṭātaṉa avviṭattut tōṉṟum eṉpatu koḷka.
32.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	yāteṉa varūum viṉāviṉ kiḷavi
	aṟinta poruḷvayi ṉaiyan tīrtaṟkut
	terinta kiḷavi yātalu murittē.
e - tu. yātu eṉṉum viṉāccol aṟiyāpporuḷai viṉāvutalēyaṉṟi aṟinta poruṭkaṇ aiyantīrtaṟku ārāynta collātalum urittu.
u - m :- nam erutu aintiṉuḷ keṭṭa erutu yātu eṉavarum.
terinta kiḷavi yātalumurittu eṉṟataṉāl namaruḷ yāvaṉ pōyiṉāṉ eṉa aintiṉōṭum oṭṭuka. kaṭi collillai eṉpataṉāl namaruḷ evaṉ pōyiṉāṉ. avaṟṟuḷ evverutu keṭṭatu eṉpaṉavum koḷka. (ṅau)
33.	iṉaitteṉa vaṟinta ciṉaimutaṟ kiḷavikku
	viṉaippaṭu tokutiyi ṉummai vēṇṭum.
40.	cuṭṭumuta lākiya kāraṇak kiḷaviyuñ
	cuṭṭuppeya riyaṟkaiyiṟ ceṟiyat tōṉṟum.
e - tu. cuṭṭuppeyar iyaṟpeyariṉ piṉ kiḷakkappaṭṭatu pōlac cuṭṭu mutalākiya kāraṇac collaiyum iyaṟ peyariṉ piṉvaittuc colluka.
u - m :- cāttaṉ kaiyeḻutumāṟu vallaṉ ataṉāṟ ṟamāciriyaṉ uvakkum cātti cāntaraikkumāṟu vallaḷ ataṉāṟ koṇṭāṉ uvakkum eṉa varum. cuṭṭu mutalākiya kāraṇak kiḷavi; itu viṉaikkoruṅkiyalum vaḻi varum eṉṟu koḷḷuka.
41.	ciṟappi ṉākiya peyarnilaik kiḷavikku
	miyaṟpeyark kiḷavi muṟpaṭak kiḷavār.
e - tu. ciṟappiṉākiya peyarkku muṉ iyaṟpeyarai vaittuc collār. ummaiyāl tavaṅ kalvi kuṭippiṟappu mutaliyaṉavum koḷka.
u - m :- 
ēṉāti nallutaṭaṉ; vāyilāṉ cāttaṉ; paṭait talaivaṉ tīraṉ; cēramāṉ cēralātaṉ; cōḻaṉ nalaṅkiḷḷi; pāṇṭiyaṉ māṟaṉ; muṉivaṉ akattiyaṉ; teyvap pulavaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvaṉ; pārppāṉ kaṇṇaṉ; vēḷāḷaṉ koṟṟaṉ; vaṇṇāṉ cāttaṉ; kōlikaṉ cāttāṉ; taccaṉ koṟṟaṉ; kollaṉ ceyyāṉ; tāvitaṉ māṟaṉ eṉa varum.	(caka)
42.	oruporuḷ kuṟitta vēṟupeyark kiḷavi
	toḻil vēṟu kiḷappiṉ oṉṟiṭaṉ ilavē.
e - tu. oru poruḷ karutivanta pala coṟkaḷukku oru toḻilē muṭipākak kūṟātu peyar tōṟum vēṟutoḻil koṭuppiṉ oru poruḷavāy oṉṟutal ilavām.
u - m :- āciriyaṉ pērūrkiḻāṉ ceyiṟṟiyaṉ iḷaṅkaṇṇaṉ cāttaṉ vantāṉ eṉṟu colluka.
iṉi āciriyaṉ vantāṉ, pērūrkiḻāṉiruntāṉ, ceyiṟṟiyaṉ kiṭantāṉ, kaṇṇaṉ pōṉāṉ, cāttaṉiṉṟāṉ eṉṉaṟka. aṉṟiyum āciriyaṉ vantāṉ, pērūr kiḻāṉ vantāṉ eṉappeyar tōṟum oruviṉai koṭuppiṉam vēṟu vēṟu poruḷām.
iṉiyittaṉmaiyaṉa koḷka
entai varuka emmāṉ varuka
maintaṉ varuka maṇāḷaṉ varuka
paintoṭip paṇait tōṭkiḻavaṉ varuka.
eṉak kātalpaṟṟip palakāl oru toḻil vantaṉa koḷka.
aṉṟiyum iṉit toḻil vēṟu kiḷappi ṉoṉṟiṭa ṉilavē eṉpataṉāl vēṟu toḻil koṭātu oru toḻilē koṭuppiṉ oṉṟiṭaṉām eṉap peyar tōṟum oru toḻil koṭuttut tutiyiṭattuc colluka.
u - m :-
	tēmala raṅkaṭ ṭiruvē pukutuka
	māmalark kaṇṇi maṇāḷaṉ putukuka
	kāmaṉ pukutuka kāḷai pukutuka
	nāma veḻilviñcai nampi pukutuka.
eṉpaṉa koḷka.
43.	taṉmaic collē aḵṟiṇaik kiḷavi yeṉ
	ṟeṇṇu vaḻi maruṅkiṉ viravutal varaiyār.
e - tu :- taṉmaic collum aḵṟiṇaic collum avai eṇṇum iṭattu virāy varutalai nīkkār.
u - m :-
	“yāṉum eṉeḵkamuñ cāṟum
	avaṉuṭaiya yāṉaikkuñ cēṉaikkum pōr 
eṉavarum.
itu itu tiṇaiyum virāyeṇṇi aḵṟiṇai uyar tiṇaiyōṭu muṭintatu. eṇṇuvaḻi maruṅku eṉṟataṉāl nīyu niṉpaṭaikkalamuñ cāṟir, avaṉuntaṉ kaḷiṟuñ cāṟum eṉa muṉṉilaiyiṉum paṭarkkaiyiṉum koḷka.
44.	orumai yeṇṇiṉ potup piripāṟ col
	orumaik kallatu yeṇṇumuṟai nillātu.
e - tu:- orumai eṇṇiṉai uṇarttum oruvar eṉṉum potumaiyiṉiṉṟum pirintu pāluṇarttum collākiya oruvaṉ orutti eṉṉum col orumaikkallatu irumai mutaliya eṇkaḷiṉ ōṭātu.
potuvāvatu - oruvar. atu irupāṟkum potu, atu piriyākkāl oruvar, iruvar, mūvar, nālvar eṉa evvaḷavum eṇ ōṭumāṟu kāṇka. appotu pirintu oruvaṉ eṉa niṉṟa piṉ iruvaṉ mūvaṉ eṉa ākātu. orutti eṉa niṉṟapiṉ irutti mutti eṉavum ākātu. ivvāṟu kūṟiyatu ṉakaravīṟum eṇṇutaṟku iyaipu iṉmaiyāṉum rakaraīṟu iyaipuṭaimai yāṉum eṉka.	(44)
45.	viyaṅkō ḷeṇṇuppeyar tiṇaiviravu varaiyār.
e - tu:- viyaṅkōḷoṭu toṭarum eṇṇuppeyar iru tiṇaiyum virāy varutalai nīkkār.
u - m :
	āvum āyaṉuñ celka; yāṉaiyum pākaṉum varuka.
eṉavarum
ummai - eṇṇummai.	(45)
46.	vēṟuviṉaip potuccol oruviṉai kiḷavār.
e - tu. vevvēṟu viṉaiyaiyuṭaiya palaporuṭkup potuvākiya collai yātāṉum oruviṉaiyāṟ kūṟār. oru viṉaiyāṟ kūṟār. eṉavē ellāvaṟṟaiyum uḷḷaṭakki niṟkum potuviṉaiyāṟ kūṟuvar eṉpatām.
u - m :-
aṭicil eṉpatu uṇpaṉa, tiṉpaṉa, nakkuvaṉa, parukuvaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
aṇi eṉpatu kaṭṭuvaṉa, kavippaṉa, ceṟippaṉa, pūṇpaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
iyam eṉpatu koṭṭuvaṉa, ūtuvaṉa, eḻūuvaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
paṭai eṉpatu eyvaṉa, eṟivaṉa, veṭṭuvaṉa, kuttuvaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
ātaliṉ aṭicil uṇṭār, aṭicil kaitoṭṭār eṉavum, aṇi aṇintār, meyppaṭuttār eṉavum, iyam iyampiṉār, paṭuttār eṉavum, paṭai vaḻaṅkiṉār, toṭṭār eṉavum potu viṉaiyāṟ kūṟuka. allatu aṭicil kuṭittār, parukiṉār eṉṟum, aṇikavittār, kaṭṭiṉār eṉṟum, iyamkoṭṭiṉār; ūtiṉār eṉṟum, paṭai eytār kuttiṉār eṉṟum oṉṟaṉ toḻilāṟ kūṟaṟka.
aṟucuvai yaṭici laṇiyiḻai tarutaliṉ
uṟuvayi ṟāra vōmpātu tiṉṟēṉ
eṉa vēṟu viṉaiyāl vantatāl eṉiṉ iḻittaṟkaṇ vantatu eṉka.	(46)
mūrkkaṉum mutalaiyuṅ koṇṭatu viṭār
naṅkaiyuṅ kiḷiyum uṇṭār.
vēntaṉ perumpati maṇṇāḷ mākka
ḷīṅkivar mūva ritaṟkuri yōrē.
vaṇṭama rōti nīyu nallai.
taṇṭār mārpi ṉivaṉum nallaṉ
olippāṭṭiṉiv vūru naṉṟē.
tīyōr yāvaru millai.
eṉa ivai tiṇaivirāy eṇṇi uyartiṇaiyāṉ muṭintavāṟu kāṇka.	(51)
52.	viṉaivēṟu paṭūum palaporu ḷorucol
	viṉaivēṟu paṭāap palaporu ḷorucoleṉ
	ṟāyiru vakaiya palaporu ḷorucol.
e - tu : -  viṉaivēṟupaṭum palaporuḷ oru collum viṉaivēṟupaṭāp pala poruḷ oru collum eṉa iraṇṭu vakaippaṭum pala poruḷ oru col.
u - m :-
avai - mā, vāḷ, kōl kaṉṟu, taḷir, pū, kāy, paḻam eṉṉum toṭakkattaṉa.
53.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	viṉaivēṟu paṭūum palaporu ḷorucol
	vēṟupaṭu viṉaiyiṉum iṉattiṉamum cārpiṉum
	tēṟat tōṉṟum poruṭeri nilaiyē.
e - tu :- viṉaivēṟu paṭūum pala poruḷ oru collai aṟiyum - iṭattu vēṟupaṭṭa viṉaiyiṉāṉum iṉattiṉāṉum cārpiṉāṉum poruḷteri nilai teḷiyat tōṉṟum.
u - m :-
mā taḷirttatu, pūttatu, kāyttatu, eṉṟa iṭattu māmarattiṉ mēlum, māvōṭu māp pāyum eṉṟa iṭattu vilaṅkiṉ mēlum cellum. (ivai) viṉaiyāṉ aṟiyap paṭṭaṉa.
iṉi, māvum maraiyum pulampaṭarntaṉa; māvum nāyum ōṭiṉa eṉṟāl iṉattāl aṟiyappaṭṭaṉa.
māvummarutum ōṅkiṉa eṉṟāl ituvum iṉattāl aṟiyappaṭṭatu.
viṟpaṟṟi niṉṟu kōṟā eṉṟa iṭattuk kaṇaikkōṉ mēlum, kutiraiyēṟi yiruntu kōṟā eṉṟa iṭattu mattikaik kōṉ mēlum palakai paṟṟi niṉṟu vāṭā eṉṟa iṭattu kaṇṭaka vāṇ mēlum cellum ivai cārpālaṟiyappaṭṭaṉa eṉakkoḷka (53)
54.	oṉṟuviṉai maruṅki ṉoṉṟit tōṉṟum
e - tu:- mēṟcoṉṉa mūṉṟu vakaiyāṉum aṉṟip potuviṉaiyālum viṉai poruntum iṭattiṟ poruntit tōṉṟuvaṉavum uḷa.
māvīḻntatu eṉṟa iṭattu iṉṉamā eṉṟu aṟiyalākātu vīḻtaṟṟoḻil ellāvaṟṟiṟkum potuvē yāyiṉum iviṭattu ikkālattu ivaṉ collukiṉṟatu immāviṉai eṉa oṉṟiṉai uṇartti niṉṟavāṟu kāṇka.
itaṉ karuttu:- viṉai vēṟupaṭum pala poruḷ oru col oru kāl viṉai vēṟupaṭāp pala poruḷ oru coṟ pōlavum niṟkum eṉpatu.
55.	viṉaivēṟu paṭāap palaporu ḷorucol
	niṉaiyuṅ kālaik kiḷantāṅ kiyalum.
e - tu:- viṉaivēṟupaṭāp palaporuḷ oru col ārāyum kālattu itu eṉak kiḷakkappaṭṭa ārāycciyuṭaiya iṭattu naṭakkum.
u - m:-
pala kaṉṟu ōriṭattu uḷvaḻik kaṉṟu nīruṭṭuka eṉṟakkāl ellāvaṟṟiṟkum nīrūṭṭum viṉaip potuvākalāṉ kaṉṟu nīrūṭṭuka eṉṟu potuvākac collātu āṉ kaṉṟai nīrūṭṭuka.
erumaik kaṉṟai nīrūṭṭuka eṉak kiḷantu colluka. marak kaṉṟu pala niṉṟuḻiyum iṉṉa kaṉṟukku nīrūṭṭuka eṉak kiḷantu kūṟuka.
niṉaiyuṅkālai eṉṟataṉāl ārāycciyillāta iṭattuk kiḷavātu colluka.
u - m :-
kaṉṟāṟṟup paṭutta puṉṟalaic ciṟāar eṉak kiḷavātu kūṟiyavāṟu kāṇka.
56.	kuṟittōṉ kūṟṟam terittu moḻikiḷavi.
e - tu:- tāṉ oru poruḷaik karutic collumēl ataṉai iṟṟeṉṟu terittuc colluka.
u - m :-
	aritārac cāntaṅ kalantatu pōla
	urukeḻut tōṉṟi varumē - murukuṟaḻum
	aṉpaṉ malaip peyta nīr eṉat terittu moḻi.
	kalantatu pōla varumē yilaṅkaruvi
	yampaṉ malaippeyta nīr eṉṟu kūṟaṟka.
aṉṟiyum,
	pallārtōḷ tōyntu varutalāṟ pāypuṉal
	nalvaya lūratiṉ ṟār pulāl-pullerukka
	mākiṉ maṇippūṇem maintaṉ malaintamaiyāṟ
	kātaṟṟāy nāṟum emakku 
eṉavum terittu moḻintavāṟu.
	ōllēṅ kuvaḷai pulāal makaṉmārpiṟ
	pullerukkaṅ kaṇṇi naṟitu
eṉṟu kūṟaṟka; kūṟil vaḻuvām. (rukū)
57.	kuṭimai āṇmai iḷamai mūppē
	aṭimai vaṉmai varuntē kuḻuvē
	peṇmai aracē makavē kuḻavi
	taṉmai tiripeyar uṟuppiṉ kiḷavi
	kātal ciṟappē ceṟaṟcol viṟaṟcoleṉ
	ṟāvaṟu mūṉṟum uḷappaṭat tokaii
	aṉṉa piṟavum avaṟṟoṭu civaṇi
	muṉṉattiṉ uṇarum kiḷavi ellām
	uyartiṇai maruṅkiṉ nilaiyiṉa āyiṉum
	aḵṟiyai maruṅkiṟ kiḷantāṅ kiyalum.
e - tu:- kuṭimai mutal viṟaṟcol īṟākac collap paṭṭa patiṉeṭṭum uṭpaṭa avaipōlvaṉa piṟavum avaṟṟoṭu tokkuk kuṟippiṉāṉ uyartiṇaip poruḷai uṇarttum coṟkaḷ ellām uyartiṇai muppāṟ kaṇṇum nilaipeṟṟaṉa. āyiṉum aḵṟiṇaip poruḷai uṇartti niṉṟuḻippōla aḵṟiṇai muṭipē koḷḷum.
u - m :-
	ivarkkuk kuṭimai naṉṟu, kuṭimai tītu,
	ivarkku eṉpataṉai yāṇṭum oṭṭik koḷka.
	āṇmai naṉṟu - tītu;
	iḷamai naṉṟu - tītu; mūppu naṉṟu - tītu,
	aṭimai naṉṟu - tītu; vaṉmai naṉṟu - tītu;
	viruntu vantatu - pōyiṟṟu; kuḻu naṉṟu, pirintatu;
	peṇmai naṉṟu - tītu,
	aracu vantatu - pōyiṟṟu; maka naṉṟu - tītu;
	kuḻavi aḻutatu - kiṭantatu
ulakamum uyirum uṭampum makkaṭ paṇpu; allaṉa teyvam eṉak koḷka.
pāl pirinticaiyā vuyartiṇai mēṉa; uyartiṇaimēṟ pāl pirinticaiyā eṉka.
59.	niṉṟāṅ kicaittal ivaṇiyal piṉṟē.
e - tu:- īṟu tiriyātu niṉṟāṅku niṉṟu uyartiṇaiyāy icaittal kālam ulakam mutaliya coṟkaḷiṭattu iyal pillai; eṉavē kuṭimai mutaliya coṟkaḷiṭattu niṉṟāṅ kicaittal iyalpām eṉpatām.
kālamulakam eṉṉum cūttiram.
u - m :-
	kuṭimai nallaṉ; āṇmai nallaṉ;
	iḷamai nallaṉ; mūpput tīyaṉ eṉa oṭṭikkoḷka.
ic cūttiram kuṭimai yāṇmai eṉṉum cūttirattukkup puṟanaṭai eṉṟaṟika.
60.	icaittalum uriya vēṟiṭat tāṉa.
e - tu:- kālam mutaliya coṟkaḷ uyartiṇaiyāy icaittalum uriya; īṟu tirintu vāypāṭu vēṟupaṭṭaviṭattu, vēṟiṭamāvatu īṟutirital.
kālam eṉpatu makaravīṟu, atu kālaṉ eṉat tirintatu. piṟavum ēṟkumāṟu tirittuc colluka.
u - m :-
kālaṉ koṇṭāṉ; ulakāppaṉ, uyirkkiḻavaṉ pōyiṉāṉ
eṉa oṭṭuka.
ic cūttiram kālamulakam eṉpataṟkup puṟanaṭai. 	(60)
61.	eṭutta moḻiiṉañ ceppalum urittē.
e - tu:- itu aruttāpatti kūṟukiṉṟatu. iṉamākiya poruṭkaṇ oṉṟai vitantu kūṟiyavaḻi accol taṉakku iṉamākiya piṟaporuḷaik kuṟippāṉ uṇarttalum urittu.
eṭutta moḻi-vitanta col.
u - m :-
aṟañ ceytāṉ tuṟakkam pukum eṉṟa vaḻi maṟañ ceytāṉ niraiyam pukum eṉpatu uṇarttiṟṟu. iḻipaṟin tuṇpāṉ iṉpam eytum eṉak kaḻipēriraiyāṉ iṉpam eytāṉ eṉpatu uṇarttiṟṟu. kuṭaṅ koṇṭāṉ vīḻntāṉ eṉak kuṭam vīḻntatu collāmal muṭintatu.
piṟavum aṉṉa.
iṉañ ceppalu murittē eṉṉum ummaiyāṟ ceppāmaiyum urittu. ā vāḻka. antaṇar vāḻka. eṉṟāl oḻintaṉavum oḻintārum cāka eṉpatu aṉṟu eṉak koḷka.
62.	kaṇṇum tōḷum mulaiyum piṟavum
	paṉmai cuṭṭiya ciṉainilaik kiḷavi
	paṉmai kūṟuṅ kaṭappā ṭilavē
	tamviṉaik kiyalum eḻuttalaṅ kaṭaiyē.
e - tu :- kaṇṇum tōḷum mulaiyum avai pōlvaṉa piṟavum paṉmaiyaik kuṟittu niṉṟa ciṉai nilaimaiyai uṇarttiya coṟkaḷ tamakku uriya paṉmai viṉaikku ēṟṟa akara īṟṟāṟ kūṟak karutātu, tammutal viṉaikku ēṟṟa orumai īṟṟāṉumpaṉmai īṟṟāṉum kūṟak karutiya vaḻit tamakku uriya paṉmaiyāṟ kūṟappaṭum muṟaimaiyuṭaiya alla.
u - m :-
kaṇnallaṉ, kaṇ nallaḷ, kaṇnallar;
tōḷnallaṉ, tōḷ nallaḷ, tōḷnallar;
mulainallaṉ (?) mulainallaḷ, mulai nallar eṉavum
kaṇ nontāṉ, mulai nontāṉ eṉavum varum.
piṟavum eṉṟataṉāṟ puruvam kātu kāl mutaliyaṉa koḷka.
paṉmai kūṟuṅ kaṭappāṭila
eṉṟataṉāl orumaikkum koḷka.
mūkku nallaḷ, koppūḻ nallaḷ eṉka. tamviṉaikkiyalu meḻuttalaṅkaṭaiyē, eṉṟataṉāl tam mutalukku iyalum eḻuttāṉ muṭiyum eṉpatām.
ic cūttiram aḵṟiṇai uyartiṇaiyāṉ muṭiyumāṟu kūṟit tiṇaivaḻuvamaittatu.
taṉṉiṉa muṭittal eṉpataṉāṟ kōṭukūriyatu. kuḷampu kūritu, kutirai eṉat tiṇaivaḻuviṉṟi aḵṟiṇaip paṉmaic ciṉaippeyar niṉṟu mutal viṉaiyākiya orumaiyāṉ muṭivaṉavum koḷka.	(62)
mutalāvatu kiḷaviyākka utāraṇam muṟṟum.


iraṇṭāvatu vēṟṟumai iyal
63.	vēṟṟumai tāmē ēḻeṉa moḻipa.
e - tu :- vēṟṟumai eṉṟu collappaṭuvaṉa ēḻu vakaiya.
vēṟṟumaiyāvatu coṟkaḷai vēṟṟumai ceyvatu. ēḻuvakaiyāvatu; cāttaṉuṇṭāṉ eṉṟa iṭattu eḻuvāyum, cāttaṉ cōṟṟai uṇṭāṉ eṉṟa iṭattu iraṇṭāvatum, cāttaṉ koṟṟaṉoṭu vantāṉ eṉa mūṉṟāvatum, cāttaṟkuk koṭuttāṉ eṉa nālāvatum, cāttaṉiṉ valiyaṉ eṉṟa iṭattu aintāvatum, cāttaṉatāṭai eṉṟa iṭattu āṟāvatum, cāttaṉiruntāṉ muṟṟattukkaṇ eṉa ēḻāvatum ām.
ippaṭiyāka vēṟṟumaippaṭu poruḷ koṇṭu varutalāl vēṟṟumai ēḻeṉappaṭṭatu.	(ka)
64.	viḷikoḷ vataṉkaṇ viḷiyō ṭeṭṭē.
e - tu:- viḷi ēṟkum peyaroṭu vēṟṟumai eṭṭām. viḷikoḷvataṉkaṇ eṉṟataṉāl viḷi elātaṉavum uḷa. avai viḷimarapiṟ kāṇka.
65.	avai tām.
	peyar ai ōṭu ku
	iṉ atu kaṇ viḷi eṉṉu mīṟṟa.
e - tu :- eṭṭu vēṟṟumaiyum viḷi vēṟṟumaiyai iṟutiyāka uṭaiya peyarum, aiyum, ōṭuvum, kuvvum, iṉṉum, atuvum, kaṇṇum, ām.
66.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	eḻuvāy vēṟṟumai peyar tōṉṟu nilaiyē.
e - tu :- eḻuvāy vēṟṟumai urupum viḷiyum elātu, peyarākavē tōṉṟu nilaimai yatuvām.
u - m :-
ā eṉa varum.
67.	poruṇmai cuṭṭal viyaṅkoḷa varutal
	viṉainilai yuraittal viṉāviṟ kēṟṟal
	paṇpukoḷa varutal peyarkoḷa varutaleṉ
	ṟaṉṟi vaṉaittum peyarppaya ṉilaiyē.
e - tu:- eḻuvāy vēṟṟumai poruṇmai cuṭṭal mutalākiya āṟuvakaippaṭṭa payaṉilaiyaiyum ēṟkum.
u - m :-
poruṇmai cuṭṭal:- āvuṇṭu eṉpatu,
viyaṅkoḷa varutal :- ā celka eṉpatu,
viṉainilaiyuraittal :- ākiṭantatu eṉpatu,
viṉāviṟ kēṟṟal :- āvō eṉpatu,
paṇpukoḷa varutal :- ākaritu eṉpatu,
peyar koḷa varutal :- ā oṉṟu, pala eṉpatu.
poruḷ eṉṉātu poruṇmai eṉṟataṉāl ā - eṉṉum poruḷ, keṭṭatēṉum av āviṉatu cātittaṉmai ek kālattum keṭātu niṟkum eṉpatu koḷka.
68.	peyariṉākiya tokaiyumā ruḷavē
	avvu muriya appā lāṉa.
e - tu :- peyarum peyarum tokka tokaiyumuḷa avaiyum uriya av eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaiyāyp payaṉilai kōṭaṟku.
u - m :-
yāṉaikkōṭu uṇṭu-celka. vīḻntatu, kōṭō, paritu, pala
eṉa varum.
ummaiyāl viṉaiyoṭu peyartokkatūum eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaiyāyp payaṉilai koḷḷum.
kolyāṉai uṇṭu - celka eṉa oṭṭikkoḷka. aṉṟiyum, tokaiyu māruḷavē eṉṟataṉāl aṟuvakaip paṭṭa tokaic collum eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaiyāyp payaṉilai koḷḷum eṉka.
u - m:-
yāṉaik kōṭu kiṭantatu, matimukam viyarttatu. kol yāṉai niṉṟatu, karuṅ kutirai yōṭiṟṟu, uvāppatiṉāṉku kaḻintatu, poṟṟoṭi vantāḷ eṉa varum.
69.	evvayiṟ peyarum veḷippaṭat tōṉṟi
	avviyal nilaiyal cevvi teṉpa.
e - tu :- taṉmai muṉṉilai paṭarkkai eṉṉum mūṉṟiṭattup peyarum viḷaṅkat tōṉṟi eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaiyāyp payaṉilai kōṭal cevvitu eṉpa. muṉ eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaip peyar iṉṉatu eṉṟu varaintatu iṉmaiyiṉ ammūṉṟiṭattup peyarumē eḻuvāy vēṟṟumaip peyar eṉṟu uṇarttiyavāṟu kāṇka.
70.	kūṟiya muṟaiyiṉ urupunilai tiriyā
	tīṟu peyarkkākum iyaṟkaiya eṉpa.
e - tu :- mēl ai, ōṭu, ku, iṉ, atu kaṇ eṉṟu kūṟiya muṟaiyaiyuṭaiya urupukaḷ tattam nilai tiriyātu peyarkku īṟām iyalpai uṭaiya eṉpa.
u - m :-
cāttaṉai, cāttaṉoṭu, cāttaṟku, cāttaṉiṉ cāttaṉatu, cāttaṉ kaṇ eṉa varum.
71.	peyarnilaik kiḷavi kālan tōṉṟā
	toḻi ṉilai yoṭṭum oṉṟalaṅ kaṭaiyē.
e - tu :- peyaratu nilaiyilē niṟṟaluṭaiya col, kālam tōṉṟātu, oru poruḷatu puṭai peyarcciyāyk kālam tōṉṟutaṟkup poruntum toḻiṟ peyar allāta iṭattu. oṭṭum - poruntum.
u - m :-
cāttaṉ eṉṟatu kālam tōṉṟātu, uṇṭāṉ eṉak kālam tōṉṟi niṉṟatu.
toḻil peyar - iṉi ōṭṭu moṉṟalaṅ kaṭaiyē eṉṟataṉāl toḻiṟ peyarāy niṟkum collum kālam tōṉṟātaṉavum uḷa.
avai uṇṭal, tiṉṟal, pūcal, vēṭṭai eṉpaṉa. avai toḻilatu puṭai peyarcci māttiram uṇarttuvatallatu kālam tōṉṟā.
iṉi ellāt toḻiṟ peyarum kālam tōṉṟum eṉpatu paṭac collutal kuṟṟam.
iṉi uṇṭāṉ tiṉṟāṉ eṉpuḻit toḻiṉilai oṭṭuvaṉa kālam tōṉṟi niṟṟalum kaṇṭukoḷka.
72.	iraṇṭākuvatē.
	aiyeṉap peyariya vēṟṟumaik kiḷavi
	evvaḻi variṉum viṉaiyē viṉaikkuṟip
	pavviru mutaliṟ ṟōṉṟu matuvē.
e - tu:- peyar, ai, oṭu, ku eṉṉum cūttirattu ai eṉap peyar peṟṟa vēṟṟumaic col iraṇṭāvatām. aḵti yāṇṭu variṉum viṉaiyum viṉaikkuṟippumākiya iraṇṭu mutaṟ kaṇṇum tōṉṟum. avai poruḷāka eṉṟavāṟu.
u - m :-
kuṭattai vaṉaintāṉ, kuḻaiyaiyuṭaiyaṉ eṉa varum
peyariya eṉpatu peyareṉpatu mutaṉilaiyāka muṭintatōr peyareccam. taṉmai uṇarttuvaṉavum peyar eṉappaṭutaliṉ ai eṉap peyariya eṉṟār.
viṉaiyē viṉaikkuṟippeṉṟārāyiṉum avaṟṟiṉ ceyappaṭuperuḷē koḷḷappaṭum.
ceyappaṭuporuḷāvatu oruviṉai ceyyum toḻi liṉai uṟuvatu.
avviru mutaliṟṟōṉṟum eṉṟa ataṉāṉ mutalātaṟkum vēṟṟumaip poruḷātaṟkum ēṟpaṉa avaiyē yākaliṉ, mutal kāraṇam, avai mutalātaṟku ēṟṟavāṟu eṉṉai eṉiṉ, ceyappaṭuporuṇ mutalāyiṉa toḻiṟkuk kāraṇamākalāṉ; āyeṭṭeṉpa toḻiṉ mutaṉilaiyē eṉṉum avaṟṟai mutaṉilai eṉṟu kūṟupa ākalāṉ; aḵtēṟ ceyappaṭu poruḷāvatu toḻiṟpayaṉuṟuvatākalāṉ kuḻai mutalāyiṉavaṟṟiṟku av ilakkaṇam ēlāmaiyiṟ ceyappaṭu poruḷ aṉmaiyāṉ viṉaikkuṟippu eṉal vēṇṭā; eṉiṉ, aṟṟaṉṟu; ammukkālamuṅ kuṟippoṭu koḷḷu meynnilaiyuṭaiya eṉṟār ākaliṟ kuṟippuc coṟ kāla moṭu tōṉṟiṟ ṟoḻiṟ col - ātalum kuḻai mutalāyiṉa toḻiṟpayaṉ uṟutalum āciriyaratu tuṇipu ātaliṉ avaiyum ceyappaṭu poruḷām eṉpatu, āyiṉ - ceyap paṭuporuṭ ṭōṉṟum eṉa amaiyum; viṉaiyē viṉaik kuṟippu eṉal vēṇṭā eṉiṉ, terinilai viṉaic ceyappaṭu poruḷaiyē kūṟiṉārō kuṟippu viṉaic ceyappaṭu poruḷum aṭaṅkap potu vakaiyāṟ kūṟiṉārō eṉṟu aiyam akalāmaiyiṉ viṉaiyē viṉaik kuṟippu eṉal vēṇṭum eṉpatu.
viṉai, viṉaik kuṟippu eṉpaṉa īṇṭu āku peyar. evvaḻi variṉum eṉṟataṉāl, pukaḻai niṟuttāṉ, pukaḻai niṟuttal, pukaḻaiyuṭaiyaṉ, pukaḻai uṭaimaivatu peyaroṭu toṭarnta vaḻiyum viṉaic ceyappaṭu poruḷum kuṟippuc ceyappaṭu poruḷum paṟṟiyē niṟkum.
ceyappaṭu poruḷ, iyaṟṟappaṭuvatum vēṟupaṭukkap paṭuvatum eytappaṭuvatum eṉa mūṉṟām.
iyaṟṟappaṭutalāvatu muṉ illataṉai uṇṭākkuvatu. vēṟu paṭuttal muṉ uḷḷataṉait tirittal, eytappaṭuvatu toḻiṟ payaṉuṟun tuṇaiyāy niṟṟal.
ivvurupai muṭittaṟku, mēṟkāppu mutaliya vāyppāṭu paṟṟi varum poruḷkaḷai im mūṉṟu kūṟṟāṉum pakukkiṉṟār, viṉaimāttiraiyum viṉaip peyarumākap pakuppar.
īṇṭu kuṭattai vaṉaintāṉ eṉṟalum mēl varum iḻai eṉṉum iyaṟṟappaṭum poruḷai.
atu eyilai iḻaittāṉ eṉa varum.
eṉṟatu eyilai yiḻaittalaic ceytāṉ eṉṉum poruṭṭu, iḻai eṉṉum viṉai māttirai uṇarttum peyar nilai, muṉṉal iḻaittal eṉṉum peyarait tōṟṟuvittu; iḻaittalai eṉa urupaiyum ēṟpittuc ceytāṉ eṉa viṉai yoḻinta kāraṇaṅkaḷ ēḻāṉum nikaḻum kāriyattiṉaiyum tōṟṟuvittu niṟṟaliṉ iḻai eṉṉum mutaṉilaip peyarai vēṟṟumaip poruḷāka eṭuttu ōtiṉār. ip poruṭkaṭ ṭōṉṟiya ceytāṉ eṉṉum kāriyattuṭa ṉallatu, eyilai eṉṉum iraṇṭāvatu muṭiyāmai uṇarka.
ivvāṟē mēl ic cūttirattil oḻinta poruḷ kaḷaiyum virikka.
iṉi viṉaik kuṟippiṟkum kuḻaiyaiyuṭaiyaṉ eṉpuḻi uṭaiyam eṉak karutal viṉai. ak karuttai nikaḻttukiṉṟāṉ viṉai mutal. ak kuḻai avaṉ karuttu nikaḻttappaṭu poruḷāyk kiṭakkiṉṟa taṉmai; ceyappaṭu poruḷ. ivvāṟē oḻintaṉavum kuṟippāṟ kāṇka.	(ya)
73.	kāppi ṉoppi ṉūrtiyi ṉiḻaiyi
	ṉōppiṟ pukaḻiṟ paḻiyi ṉeṉṟā
	peṟali ṉiḻaviṟ kātaliṉ vekuḷiyiṟ
	ceṟali ṉuvattaliṟ kaṟpi ṉeṉṟā
	aṟuttaliṟ kuṟaittaliṟ ṟokuttaliṟ pirittali
	ṉiṟuttali ṉaḷavi ṉeṇṇi ṉeṉṟā
	vākkaliṟ cārtaliṟ celaviṟ kaṉṟali
	ṉōkkali ṉañcaliṟ citaippi ṉeṉṟā
	vaṉṉavum piṟavu mammutaṟ poruḷi
	leṉṉa kiḷaviyu mataṉpāla veṉmaṉār.
e - tu :- ceyappaṭu poruṇ mūṉṟaṉaiyum paṟṟivarum vāypāṭukaḷai virikkiṉṟār.
